## M6 equipment and totems — v0.6.0 candidate protocol

### Immutable baseline — 2026-08-12

- Feature branch: `feat/equipment-totems`, created cleanly from immutable `origin/main` `a238c3e1d10d4205aa08778213bcfda846d6887f` (merge of PR #5).
- GitHub state at implementation start: releases/tags exist only through v0.4.0; M5 is merged, but no v0.5.0 tag or release exists.
- Release-state refresh on 2026-08-12: while M6 implementation was in progress, GitHub pre-release `v0.5.0` was published from `main` commit `a238c3e1d10d4205aa08778213bcfda846d6887f`. M6 neither created nor modified that tag/release. No `v0.6.0` tag or release exists.
- Installed game baseline reconfirmed: Duckov `2.3.30`, Steam build `24013657`, Unity `2022.3.62f2`; TeamSoda core SHA-256 `298d5d5885427632d5a94b2f3ce587f8ebc9528ec71e575a475158c326ecae8f`; ItemStatsSystem SHA-256 `a276e15c022f71b2214bd05e1b9b0f2e620c16561df576fed0b79c2fe4402e60`; Harmony `2.4.1.0` SHA-256 `353daafec180bb8e7bbe4da78f2a7cdc78067392e3a4e79dc8e7af295f2371e6`.
- The unmodified baseline passed 219/219 Release tests, the installed-game contract probe, native Release build with zero warnings/errors, and package verification.

### Native truth contract

- Direct equipment comes only from the exact main character's public `CharacterItem.Slots`; stable slot keys and `Item.TypeID` values determine identity.
- Selection comes from `CurrentHoldItemAgent` plus the public main-character hold-item event. A selected item must be the same object as a current direct character-slot content; otherwise selection is cleared. Slot identity is persisted so identical weapon types in different slots remain distinguishable.
- Attachment metadata is a bounded recursive read of each equipped item's public slot tree. The canonical signature uses slot keys and child `TypeID` values, not runtime instance IDs or localized names.
- The exact `Totem` tag proves totem identity. A direct slotted totem with usable durability is proven active by the verified public item-effect control flow; depleted durability proves inactive.
- Only built-in Tote Bag `Item.TypeID` 1255 items in the exact main duck's public ordinary `CharacterItem.Inventory` are Tote containers. A tagged Totem plugged into the Tote's public `AnyThing` slot proves contained presence. A Tote Bag is not a character-slot item, and it has no attached `Item.Inventory` content contract. Presence does not prove which individual effects or modifiers opted into inventory activation, so tote-carried activation is `Unknown`, the tote-activation capability is `DisabledIncompatible`, and tote contents are excluded from active-totem-set duration.
- Durations use M3 monotonic active raid time. Pause/loading intervals contribute no equipment duration. A 0.2-second bounded reconciliation supplements native change callbacks without using frame count as time. Loss or replacement of the exact main duck closes the prior equipment interval at the observation timestamp; terminal run completion closes it again idempotently, so selected state cannot remain open after invalidation or run end.
- Per-run transition history retains at most 256 latest transitions while duration aggregates remain complete. The panel retains each run's ordered loadout-duration summary. Lifetime recurring-loadout and recurring proven-active-totem-set rankings include only signatures seen in at least two completed runs; interrupted runs retain their summaries but do not establish recurrence.
- Totem presence duration is stored separately from proven-active totem-set duration. Direct active/inactive and tote `Unknown` states remain explicit, and deterministic `copy:N` rows preserve multiplicity for indistinguishable duplicate totems without inventing instance identity.
- Firing and combat events carry immutable event-time equipment associations. These are temporal associations, not causal item/totem attribution. Projectile damage, kills, hits, and completion retain the association captured at projectile initialization, so a later equipment change cannot rewrite them. Unscoped direct health damage uses the `Health.Hurt` prefix association. `CharacterBuffManager.AddBuff` snapshots the proven application association for the instantiated buff's bounded tick/update triggers; later ticks reuse that origin across equipment changes, while missing, conflicting, or cross-run origins remain explicitly unavailable instead of being recaptured or guessed.

### Automated acceptance

The M6 implementation must pass all of the following before deployment:

1. Repeated identical snapshots are idempotent; real slot, selection, attachment, and active-totem changes produce one state transition.
2. Active duration advances only through monotonic active time and survives checkpoint, interrupted recovery, normal completion, merge, JSON round-trip, and export.
3. Transition history truncates at 256 without losing aggregate duration.
4. Selected identity includes the exact slot and clears when the held item is no longer directly slotted.
5. Event-time associations remain attached to firing/damage/projectile outcomes even if later equipment differs.
6. Tote presence never upgrades activation. Duplicate tote contents retain multiplicity in presence-time rows but never accrue proven-active set time. The native capability matrix keeps tote activation `DisabledIncompatible`.
7. Schema-5 and older profiles preserve all M1-M5 values and initialize historical M6 data unavailable. Invalid equipment checkpoint counters/durations are rejected before backup selection; missing fields, canonical-key collisions, invalid enum/capability states, and malformed nested rows normalize deterministically with repair provenance. Repeated normalization is idempotent.
8. Lifetime loadout and proven-active-totem-set occurrence counts increase once per completed run; ranked recurring presentation requires at least two occurrences. Interrupted runs do not establish recurrence.
9. UI/JSON/CSV expose the same canonical item, selection, loadout, totem-presence, proven-active-set, and association totals. The panel includes recent per-run loadouts. The export contains exactly 15 files, adding `equipment_totals.csv`, `recurring_loadouts.csv`, and `equipment_combat.csv`.
10. Installed-game contract probe covers character-slot/hold/ordinary-inventory events, `CharacterItem`, `CurrentHoldItemAgent`, item-tree events, slot keys/content, tags, and ordinary inventory. Tote content specifically uses built-in type `1255` plus its public `AnyThing` slot; no attached Tote inventory contract is claimed.
11. Native Release build has zero warnings/errors; formatting, `git diff --check`, source-safety checks, package validation, checksum verification, independent extraction, and forbidden-DLL audit pass.
12. Duration, combat-association, transition, and occurrence counters saturate instead of overflowing; selected state requires a proven weapon-kind item in the exact current slot; mutable display names and runtime-object reuse do not create canonical identities.

Initial implementation checkpoint: 234/234 Release tests, the installed-game contract probe, warning-free native Release build, and package-root validation pass. This is not final delivery evidence; the exact committed head/package must be revalidated after documentation and review.

### User-controlled manual gameplay matrix

Codex may deploy only after explicit approval and only while Duckov is closed. The user launches and controls the game. Before gameplay, capture the selected UDS generation/profile and deployment hashes; do not read or modify a Duckov save unless the user selects and authorizes it.

1. Cold-launch with Harmony and UDS active. Outside a raid, confirm all M6 presence/selection capabilities are `Supported` and tote activation is explicitly `DisabledIncompatible`.
2. Enter a raid with at least one primary/secondary/melee weapon and ordinary armor/equipment. Wait long enough for measurable active time, pause for a measured interval, resume, and later verify the pause added no equipment duration.
3. Switch between at least two weapon slots. If two identical weapon types are available, use both to prove slot-qualified selection; otherwise verify distinct weapon/slot associations.
4. Equip/unequip or replace an ordinary equipment item. Change one weapon attachment if safely available. Record the action order and approximate active intervals.
5. Equip a direct `Totem`, wait, then remove it. If a depleted-durability totem is safely available, also prove it is inactive; otherwise leave that optional case unclaimed.
6. Put a `Totem` in a Tote Bag, remove it, and if practical compare a visible buff/effect. Regardless of observed gameplay, the candidate must persist tote presence as activation `Unknown` and must not add it to active-totem-set time. Concrete repeatable effect evidence is recorded for future work only; it does not enable the default-disabled capability in this candidate.
7. Fire and deal/receive damage before and after an equipment or selection change. Extract or die normally. Verify per-run associations reflect the equipment state at each event and no later change rewrote earlier rows.
8. Complete a second run using one repeated loadout and one different loadout. Verify only the repeated loadout enters lifetime recurring-loadout ranking.
9. Reopen the panel outside raids and compare item/selection/totem-set durations, transitions, and capabilities with the action log. Interrupted/integrity semantics must remain unchanged.

11. Restart Duckov and verify persistence, generation continuity, no duplicate transitions/runs, and clean adapter reacquisition. Finish with a normal shutdown and confirm no active-run/session/temp/deployment residue.

The already-trusted healing `AddBuff` postfix also forwards refresh reconciliation to combat only after its own patch-set validation succeeds. The exact `Effect.SetItem(Item)` metadata contract is added to the installed-game probe. ### Review correction — current-schema parent and checkpoint completeness

Final-candidate head `52b93bf4382554b69697a600567f25c59d52ca31`, its ZIP, and its deployment are superseded by two additional persistence findings. A schema-6 profile missing the `Statistics` or `RunTotals` parent created a default equipment aggregate without repair provenance, allowing current-capability fallback. A schema-6 active-run primary missing the equipment root, missing the retained transition list, or carrying a one-sided selected-weapon transition could be normalized only after atomic candidate selection and thereby suppress a valid backup.

Migration now remembers whether either current-schema parent root was absent before constructing defaults and marks the resulting lifetime equipment aggregate repaired. Production-path migration-to-view/export regressions cover both missing `Statistics` and missing `RunTotals`; both preserve `WasRepairedFromInvalidState`, remain non-empty for fallback purposes, and project `DisabledIncompatible` with repair provenance even when current native capabilities are supported.

Equipment checkpoint recovery validation is now schema-aware. Schema 6 requires the equipment root, all aggregate collections, the retained transition list, and complete capability members before normalization. Current-schema transitions must retain their loadout/totem identities and either both selected-weapon identity fields or neither. Schema-5 and older checkpoints may still omit M6 state and follow the historical-unavailable migration path. Three real atomic-store regressions place each malformed schema-6 primary ahead of a valid five-second backup and prove the backup is selected without repair. The focused equipment/persistence suite passes 52/52; the full Release suite passes 268/268; the installed-game contract probe, zero-warning native build, changed-file formatter verification, diff check, and package-root validation pass. These are Core persistence corrections and do not alter native observation behavior, so the accepted gameplay matrix remains applicable and no additional raid is required.

## M7 validation — unique containers successfully accessed

M7 starts from refreshed `origin/main` at `a5c46ad419d47846ecefc61079ff0f196d69907e`; the merged M6 release commit and `v0.6.0` tag both identify `b9dfffed46f29b2ff5adacaba976ce01f06bbe34`. The implementation branch is `feat/containers`. The installed validation target is Duckov `2.3.30`, Steam build `24013657`, Unity `2022.3.62f2`, and Harmony `2.4.1.0`.

The installed native contract was inspected before implementation. `InteractableLootbox.OnStartLoot` is raised only by the successful `StartLoot()` boundary after the interaction timer finishes and after a non-null inventory is available; proximity, prompt, locked, cancelled, and failed attempts do not raise it. The inherited protected `InteractableBase.interactCharacter` field supplies the actor, and the private `InteractableLootbox.GetKey()` method supplies the same stable position-derived key used by Duckov's inventory cache. Enemy corpses use the same lootbox class, so a separately owned Harmony bridge scopes `CharacterMainControl.OnDead` through `InteractableLootbox.CreateFromItem`; persisted bodies and player tombs are also classified through exact `GameplayDataSettings.Prefabs.LootBoxPrefab_Tomb` identity. Runtime object IDs are never persisted.

The pre-gameplay contract probe passes against the installed assemblies and confirms the public success event, exact actor field, stable-key method, enemy-death/create bridge, and tomb-prefab contract. The native Release adapter build also passes with zero warnings and zero errors. The inspected native files are:

### First live run and corrective findings — 2026-08-12

The first user-controlled slot-6 Ground Zero run on the initial candidate is retained as failed diagnostic evidence, not accepted M7 evidence. The user recalled three ordinary containers, two killed ducks with one freshly killed corpse looted, a prior-run grave looted, and two attempts to cancel a separate opening before its first item was revealed. The run extracted normally, exported 16 files, and closed cleanly. UDS persisted run `240f7b5373be4221b972df05681a0d82` once with four unique keys and `Supported` run capability; run, map, lifetime, JSON, `containers.csv`, and `runs.csv` all agreed on four. A fourth count is not by itself proof of corpse leakage because `OnStartLoot` fires when the loot interface becomes accessible, before item inspection/reveal; the attempted cancellation may therefore have crossed M7's successful-access boundary. The attempt is inconclusive for the negative-interaction case.

The run nevertheless exposed a definite reducer defect: keys were appended in observation order while checkpoint normalization canonicalized them by sorting. A valid descending key sequence therefore set repair provenance, and the real run, map, and lifetime aggregates became repaired/unavailable even though no persisted corruption occurred. The corrective reducer now performs binary-search deduplication and inserts every accepted key directly in sorted order. A descending-key production regression proves the checkpoint remains canonical without repair. Persisted saturation also requires the complete bounded key set and an unavailable capability; excluded base, wrong-actor, inactive-run, and corpse accesses are rejected before `GetKey()` is consulted; repaired-empty aggregates cannot inherit current supported capability in UI or exports; unexpected runtime observation and patch-verification failures become inert and unavailable without escaping into Duckov.

Bounded live diagnostics now record accepted versus reducer-rejected stable keys and independently record corpse exclusion provenance as either `character-death-scope` or `native-tomb-prefab`. This makes the corrected retest able to distinguish a genuine fourth successful interface access from a missed enemy-corpse or prior-run-grave exclusion without persisting runtime object identities.

### Review correction — checkpoint completeness and saturation persistence

Two supplied findings were reproduced against draft-PR head `072c6c56afcb14f05bc93f19f50efbb2f3e1a108` with failing production-path regressions. First, atomic recovery normalized a schema-7 `ContainerState` whose required nested roots were absent, accepted the resulting zero primary, and suppressed a valid two-access backup. Recovery-candidate validation now runs before normalization and requires current-schema `Statistics`, capability metadata, metric availability, and `LootedContainerKeys`. Five primary-versus-backup cases independently omit each required root or both principal roots and prove the intact five-second backup wins over the malformed eight-second primary with its supported count of two preserved. The same completeness gate protects new active-run writes; schema-6 and older checkpoints retain their explicit historical-unavailable migration path.

Second, the 4,097th distinct key changes a run from supported to saturated/unavailable without incrementing its 4,096 count. `RunLifecycleTracker.RecordContainer` now detects that one-time saturation transition independently of reducer acceptance and dirties the shared one-second mutation checkpoint. The regression first marks the prior 4,096-key checkpoint saved, observes that the 4,097th call still returns false, then proves a checkpoint is required and carries the complete bounded key set, `DeduplicationSaturated=true`, unchanged count, and `DisabledIncompatible` capability. Later already-saturated callbacks remain non-mutating and do not create redundant dirtiness.

Focused container/persistence coverage passes 99/99 and the full Release suite passes 303/303. Changed-file formatter verification, `git diff --check`, the installed Duckov contract probe, zero-warning native Release build, source/package safety, and five-file package validation pass. Both corrections are Core checkpoint-selection/persistence behavior and do not alter the already accepted native observation boundary or gameplay semantics, so no additional user-controlled raid is required. Exact corrective committed-head ZIP, checksum, deployment readback, pushed PR head, and CI evidence are recorded externally after the immutable corrective commit to avoid self-referential artifact metadata.

### Review correction — independent runtime capability publication

The supplied profile-write failure finding was traced on corrective draft-PR head `9d091de90e087fa5769657bbab82c1b49053cfb9`. `NativeContainerAdapter.Publish` invoked `NativeProfileCoordinator.SetContainerCapabilities` before the active-run capability handler, so an exception while `ProfileRepository.SetCapabilities` saved the profile prevented the active run from being restricted even though `DisableRuntime` had already made the native adapter inert. Publication now snapshots both payloads, independently attempts the profile and active-run destinations, and only then rethrows the original single failure or aggregates two failures. Runtime-disable diagnostics therefore remain visible without allowing a failing profile sink to leave the current run falsely supported.

The regression links the production `NativeContainerAdapter` publication partial into the test assembly, injects an `IOException` from the first/profile destination, and proves that exact exception is preserved while both the profile and active-run destinations are attempted exactly once. The focused regression passes 1/1, combined container/persistence coverage passes 100/100, and the full Release suite passes 304/304. Changed-file formatter verification, `git diff --check`, installed Duckov contract probe, zero-warning native Release build, source/package safety, and five-file package validation pass. This is a deterministic publication-failure correction with no change to native hook selection or container counting semantics; reproducing a storage failure during a live raid is neither required nor safer than the injected production-path regression. Exact committed-head artifact, deployment, pushed PR, and CI evidence are recorded externally after the immutable corrective commit.

## M8 validation — multi-map runs and route attribution

### 2026-08-13 native discovery and automated candidate evidence

Static inspection of installed Duckov 2.3.30 (Steam build 24013657, Unity 2022.3.62f2) proved the M8 boundary without temporary gameplay instrumentation:

- `RaidUtilities.NewRaid()` changes the native ID and invokes `OnNewRaid`; `LevelManager.HandleRaidInitialization()` calls it only when the prior raid ended. Non-terminal full-scene and subscene transitions therefore retain the expedition ID and do not emit a new-raid boundary.
- `SceneLoader.LoadScene` sets loading before `onStartedLoadingScene`, replaces scenes, finishes loading, waits for `LevelManager.LevelInited`, then reaches after-initialize. `LevelManager.InitLevel` replaces the main duck as needed and later calls the exact main duck's `SetPosition` before `OnAfterLevelInitialized`.
- `MultiSceneCore.ActiveSubSceneID` is the stable visited subscene ID; `MainSceneID` is only the root. `LoadSubScene` changes the active identity and fires `OnSubSceneLoaded` before `LoadAndTeleport` performs exact main-duck placement, so that event alone is not destination-ready evidence.
- Destination entry requires a proven stable map ID, initialization complete, the exact alive main duck's position callback, and restored `InputManager.InputActived`. Loading belongs to no segment. A changed raid ID outside a pending transition is a genuine interruption/new expedition.
- The installed contract probe now requires `ActiveSubSceneID` in addition to all existing full-scene, subscene, map, input, and position members. M8 adds no Harmony patches.

The schema-8 implementation uses 64-segment and 2,048-association defensive bounds. It keeps historical `RunTotals.Maps` and duration records in starting-map complete-run scope, adds separate route-map totals, and never reconstructs a pre-M8 route. Current-schema route roots and stable segment/association join keys are validated before normalization and atomic candidate selection. M1-M7 overall, starting-map, segment, and route-map reducers are independent sinks; composition assertions cover duration, movement, item/healing, firing, combat, equipment, and containers without double-counting lifetime totals. If an otherwise valid delayed outcome occurs while no destination segment is proven, its overall statistic remains available while event attribution and route-map totals become explicitly unavailable.

Focused M8 production-path tests pass 30/30. The complete Release suite passes 334/334; the installed-game native probe passes; the native Release solution builds with zero warnings and errors; changed-file formatting, analyzers, `git diff --check`, source safety, and exact five-file package validation pass. The final hardened pre-acceptance ZIP is 206,436 bytes at SHA-256 `e276698b7f468b077b9bf7f410e5ea78b2f826be1783923f725d1bd3b994e5ff`; its lowercase, two-space, LF sidecar is exact. Independent extraction to `artifacts/audit-v080-preaccept-final2-20260813T0854207438946Z` passes package validation and is byte-identical to the package root. The Core and native DLLs are 339,968 and 195,584 bytes, respectively, both report file version `0.8.0.0`, and carry the expected uncommitted-candidate product revision `0.8.0+3e16daddce317596f551f312be1f362b2e18d2eb`. The final immutable-head package is rebuilt after gameplay acceptance and commit.

### User-controlled M8 gameplay matrix

The automated contract covers unchanged single-map behavior; ordered multi-map and repeated-map routes; duplicate/reordered callbacks; exact segment timing; pause/loading exclusion; separate movement categories; event-time M1-M7 attribution; delayed source/outcome identity; terminal and interrupted recovery paths; bounds; migration; explicit historical unavailability; capability degradation; export consistency; and all M0-M7 regressions. Additional focused persistence/export assertions are added whenever review or gameplay evidence exposes a missing boundary.

After the automated, native-contract, build, package, and deployment gates pass, the user-controlled gameplay matrix is:

1. Complete a normal single-map run and verify its explicit one-segment route does not change established totals or records.
2. Complete a two-map extraction and verify start map, end map, ordered route, segment boundaries, active durations, physical distances, excluded transition displacement, and final outcome.
3. Complete a three-map expedition when practical and verify every segment remains ordered and distinct.
4. Die after transitioning to a later map and verify the death belongs to the final segment while the complete run retains its original starting map.
5. Re-enter a previously visited map when possible and verify it creates another ordered segment instead of merging non-contiguous visits.
6. Perform representative item use/healing, firing/combat, equipment, and container actions on different maps and verify their event-time segments.
7. If a safe reproducible case exists, produce one delayed healing or damage result whose source and outcome occur on different segments, then verify both identities independently.
8. Interrupt and restart after at least one completed transition, then verify exact-once recovery of completed/current segments and aggregate statistics.
9. Reopen the panel, export, and close normally. Compare the Runs UI, profile, JSON, flattened route/segment CSVs, diagnostics, checkpoint cleanup, and deployed package hashes.

The assistant backs up relevant saves/statistics before progressed-save testing, deploys only with approval while Duckov is closed, and inspects the resulting evidence. Only the user launches and controls Duckov. No historical route is reconstructed from the legacy singular `MapId`; pre-M8 ending maps, routes, segments, and route-aware per-map attribution remain explicitly unavailable.

Read-only inspection of the installed serialized scene assets refines the practical topology for matrix item 3. Build scene `level14` (`Level_GroundZero_1`) contains the `UI_Interact_ToHiddenWarehouse` transition targeting `Level_HiddenWarehouse_Main` / `Level_HiddenWarehouse` at `TeleportTargets/Entry_FromGroundZero`. Build scene `level19` (`Level_HiddenWarehouse`) contains `UI_Interact_ToFarm`, targeting `Level_Farm_Main` / `Level_Farm_01` at `TeleportTargets/FromHiddenWarehouse`. Farm's main scene (`level11`) also retains `TeleportTargets/JLabEntry`. This proves that Nullpunkt → Lagerbereich → Farmstadt is a concrete native three-map chain, subject to the progressed save's interaction unlocks. No Farm scene contains an equivalent serialized target back to Lagerbereich, so repeated-map re-entry is not assumed practical from this route and remains optional only if the game exposes another legitimate return path.

For matrix item 8 on Windows, `Alt+F4`, the window close control, and Task Manager's **End task** do not qualify as abrupt termination: prior Duckov tests proved that they can deliver Unity `OnApplicationQuit`/`OnDestroy`, allowing UDS to finalize and remove its live checkpoints cleanly. After the destination map is fully controllable and at least one action has been checkpointed, the user must run `taskkill /F /IM Duckov.exe` from a separate terminal. The `/F` is required. Do not relaunch until Codex has captured the retained active-run primary/backup and `session.json` evidence.

The five deployed gameplay-candidate files remain unchanged and valid; Core/native DLL hashes are `1cb49d6e9cecda39577b6919bdbd9bd0a88d92d7b631bccb460adb2c9290822e` and `cb7884caa03f32b6a43787c9108498121072636cc514e8383acbfc084fffa3f9`, both version `0.8.0.0`, with no deployment residue. This passes matrix items 3 and 5 and adds a seven-segment Died/export/shutdown case to items 6 and 9. The complete user-controlled M8 gameplay matrix is accepted; only final committed-head artifact/deployment and cold-start readback remain.

With Duckov still closed, Codex copied the complete accepted `Saves` and `UltimateDuckovStatistics` trees to private read-only backup `M8-routes-post-three-map-20260813T1641213890840Z` before committed-head delivery. All 485 files (34,093,069 bytes) match their sources by relative path, length, and SHA-256 and are read-only. The backed slot-1 profile hash is `64b356e9284910d211bc0df7d7b777165d0e2a028dedf5d7331a1158b3b69e78`, exactly matching the accepted live profile. No source save, profile, or prior backup was modified, restored, or deleted.

The post-gameplay pre-commit gate passes all 360 Release tests, the exact installed-game contract probe, a native Release build with zero warnings and zero errors, and five-file package validation. All 39 changed C# files pass formatter verification, `git diff --check` passes, and the tracked tree contains zero DLL/EXE/PDB files and zero forbidden game, Unity, framework, or Harmony dependencies.

### PR review hardening — 2026-08-13

Three post-delivery review findings were reproduced and corrected on the production paths. Top-level profile loading now applies a non-mutating semantic candidate validator before migration: an incomplete schema-8 primary, including a missing `RunTotals.RouteMaps` root, loses to an intact backup before any normalization or save can replace provenance-bearing route totals. Historical runs retain their original schema marker and remain valid inside a complete schema-8 profile; future top-level/statistics schemas still reach the existing unsupported-schema archive path.

Accepted firing callbacks now perform only in-memory reduction and dirty marking. Successful active-run checkpoint serialization is scheduled through the existing one-second crash-safety cadence, so 99 additional simulated automatic-fire mutations inside the interval cannot cause another growing-route clone, flush, or atomic replacement; failed writes retain the independently bounded one-second retry behavior. Orderly shutdown, route transitions, explicit teleports, terminal events, and periodic checkpoints continue to flush through their existing safety paths.

Accepted item-use publication now attempts the lifetime profile and active run independently and reports success when either accepted destination records the event. A simulated failed profile persistence result still records the active-run item use, preserving its segment association and eventual route-map contribution; the success result also keeps subsequent healing attribution attached to that accepted use. Four focused production-path regressions initially passed. The complete Release gate passes 365/365 after adding compatibility coverage for future schemas and historical per-run schema provenance; the installed-game probe, native build, and exact package validation also pass.

The follow-up profile-recovery audit broadened that semantic boundary from the reported `RouteMaps` member to the complete current-schema data contract. Before migration, a schema-8 candidate is now recursively rejected when any normally emitted `[DataMember]` root is missing at the profile, lifetime item, run-total, starting-map, route-map, record, completed-run, segment, or nested item/weapon/combat/equipment/container/capability level. Explicitly optional `EmitDefaultValue = false` members remain optional, older profiles remain eligible for migration, future profiles remain eligible for unsupported-schema archival, and deliberately repairable null weapon/ammunition identity rows still reach normalization. Eighty-seven parameterized recovery regressions remove roots including `RunTotals.Maps`, prove that the intact revision-7 backup defeats the incomplete revision-8 primary, and verify the preserved starting-map sentinel again after repository readback. The complete Release suite passes 452/452; the installed-game contract probe, zero-warning native build, changed-file formatting, `git diff --check`, and exact package validation pass. A read-only invocation of the new validator against every live and archived UDS `profile.json` and `.bak` found both current schema-8 primary/backup pairs valid and left all older schema-1/6 profiles migration-eligible.

A second recovery follow-up removed the validator's blanket null-dictionary-row exception. Null rows are now eligible only when the owning aggregate and exact property have an explicit normalizer: weapon/ammunition identities, combat breakdowns, equipment duration/association rows, and derived item-statistic groups. Null rows in profile items/groups, starting-map totals, route-map totals, duration-record maps, or item-statistic items reject the primary before migration. Six real atomic-store regressions cover those non-repairable families, including the exact `RunTotals.Maps` dereference failure, and prove backup selection plus post-open sentinel persistence; a complementary regression covers all four repairable families and the established weapon identity-repair tests remain green. The complete suite passes 459/459 before committed-head release validation.

## M8.1 validation — performance investigation and hot-path hardening

During an M8 gameplay build before review hardening, sustained firing with the Vektor SMG appeared to reduce an approximately 200+ FPS baseline to roughly 100-150 FPS even when firing into empty space. Enemy hits made the slowdown worse, and perceived frame-time spikes were substantially more severe than the Steam overlay's averaged FPS suggested. Controls with the review-hardened merged M8 build reproduced material drops with the Vektor SMG, Electrified MP7, and MF assault rifle. The evidence therefore identifies a broader firing-related problem, with additional degradation when hits add combat activity; it does not identify the Vektor itself as the cause. This activates M8.1, but it does not yet prove the remaining UDS contribution or that M8 introduced the problem: earlier milestones did not include a UDS-disabled comparison or an equivalent sustained-firing stress matrix across multiple weapons, so the game-only baseline, introduction point, and responsible subsystem remain unknown.

Review identified one concrete M8 hot path: every accepted shot synchronously requested an active-run checkpoint after M8 made that checkpoint clone the growing route and association set before durable atomic JSON replacement. The merged correction keeps accepted firing mutations in memory and schedules successful persistence through the existing bounded one-second checkpoint cadence; failed writes retain a separately bounded retry, and transition, terminal, shutdown, and periodic safety paths still flush. The persistent live regression proves that correction was necessary but insufficient. M8.1 must profile the cumulative M1-M8 runtime and isolate item/healing, lifecycle/movement, firing/projectiles, combat, equipment, containers, route fan-out, persistence/checkpointing, diagnostics, and UI before choosing further changes.

The M8.1 branch freezes its raw-frame method and acceptance calculation in `PERFORMANCE.md`. The capture wrapper verifies CapFrameX, A/B/C/D activation, and production C/D versions and hashes, records the complete control sidecar, and refuses evidence overwrite. A dependency-free analyzer requires raw frame rows, skips the CapFrameX marker, selects the dominant swap chain, uses CapFrameX's QPC-millisecond clock for action correlation, reports both whole-capture and isolated action-window distributions plus spike offsets, detects rolling one-second >33.3 ms clusters, aggregates medians of independent runs, and directly flags a repeatable action-window cluster present in at least two thirds of three or more candidate captures and absent from matched controls. Eight deterministic analyzer regressions cover CapFrameX preamble handling, distribution/threshold calculation, isolated action-window calculation, dominant-swap-chain selection, QPC millisecond normalization, millisecond-scaled fallback timestamps, action correlation, cluster semantics, per-run aggregation, control comparison, production-versus-diagnostic separation, and rejection of FPS-only input. Compile-time diagnostic counters separately expose firing/projectile/health/equipment/Harmony-inspection/checkpoint call volume and emit one shutdown summary; two regressions prove independent counts and bounded logging, and IL decompilation confirms ordinary production callers contain zero diagnostic counter calls while the opt-in build contains all 17 current boundary call sites. The complete post-action-window Release suite passes 469/469, including all 11 performance-category checks; the installed-game contract probe passes Duckov 2.3.30 build 24013657 and Harmony 2.4.1, the analyzer and native DLL build with zero warnings/errors, capture scripts parse, and the normal exact-five-file package verifies. The earlier pre-pilot diagnostic package also verified; no diagnostic or candidate bytes have been deployed.

The controlled matrix promotes the retained Electrified MP7 to the primary high-rate weapon because the Vektor used in the historical observation was lost during that run. The benchmark does not modify the save or recreate inventory to recover it; a distinct available automatic and the slower cross-class control remain required. The first attempted A launch on 2026-08-13 was rejected before capture: `Player.log` reported both `ModActive_*` flags as false but proved `Mod Loaded: HarmonyLoadMod`, `Mod Loaded: UltimateDuckovStatistics`, and `[UDS] activated`. The wrapper now fails closed on actual load and activation evidence instead of accepting those unreliable configuration flags. No raw frame artifact from that invalid launch is retained as benchmark evidence.

The user froze the primary pilot control as the Electrified MP7 with Rost-Muni S, 30 rounds loaded and 286 in reserve, at 2560×1440 borderless fullscreen, 240 Hz, 240 FPS limit, V-sync off, FSR 1.0 off, and all remaining graphics settings at their highest High/On value. Screenshots independently show the 2560×1440 borderless mode and the V-sync/limit/FSR/high-or-on graphics controls. This active launch intentionally loaded Harmony and UDS and is therefore discarded before any A capture; the user will deactivate both and cold-restart.

The loadout screenshot freezes the unchanged 97/97-durability weapon instance with occupied optic, MP muzzle, and vertical-grip slots and empty tactical and magazine-modification slots. The optic and muzzle localized labels are truncated in the native panel; their slot occupancy and the retained instance are authoritative, and no speculative full name is recorded. The attachments remain installed for every matched configuration.

The subsequent cold launch started at `2026-08-13T21:09:06.7185681Z` as process 35836. The hardened wrapper's non-capturing preflight accepted configuration A with zero configured-active mods, zero actually loaded mods, and no UDS activation evidence. This proves the clean launch state without consuming ammunition or creating a raw capture; positioning and the synchronized action still require user confirmation.

Two direct collectors then failed before creating evidence: the standalone FrameView SDK PresentMon process exited 1 for both the attempted firing run and an idle validation, while the FrameView SDK service client could not establish a usable session. The first attempt may have consumed one MP7 magazine but produced no CSV or sidecar and is objectively invalid. The fallback-started manual `FvSvc` later conflicted with CapFrameX; an elevated exact-service stop left it Stopped, with no matching process or ETW session. Nothing was uninstalled.

Frame correlation identifies the remaining first-candidate phase rather than guessing from the aggregate. The largest ordinary action frames recur about 2.1 seconds apart (for example, 11.001 ms at 5.71 seconds and 9.291 ms at 7.84 seconds in run 3), and the same phase continues outside firing. The diagnostic build counted 260 idle and 272 firing-interval patch-set inspections; ordinary periodic coverage explains about 180 inspections per 30 seconds, while the directly equipped `Totem: Heilung III` supplies frequent effect/health callbacks. Source tracing then confirmed that those callbacks synchronously called `ReflectiveHarmonyPatcher.IsPatchSetTrusted`, violating the frozen no-reflection-per-health-tick gate even after the first candidate distributed periodic checks.

The second correction retains activation-time and periodic patch-set validation but removes this work from both hot paths. Healing callback trust is now a bounded cached-registration lookup. Healing, combat, and container integrity checks submit one serialized single-flight background inspection at the existing distributed cadence and only poll its completed result from `Update`; cleanup drains the worker before exact-owner unpatching, and untrusted or failed results still disable the affected capability rather than continuing with ambiguous evidence. UDS Harmony patch/read/unpatch access is serialized across these workers and lifecycle operations. Two focused worker tests prove non-blocking polling, rejected duplicate submission, trusted/untrusted detail preservation, exception surfacing, draining, and reuse after completion. The complete Release suite passes 480/480, the native Release build and frame-time analyzer build pass with zero warnings/errors, compiled decompilation proves `NativeHealingAttributionAdapter.IsPatchPointTrusted` contains only the capability/registration-cache checks and no `IsPatchSetTrusted` call, and the ordinary DLL contains zero diagnostic invocation sites. Candidate-2 packaging and native D repetition remain required; none of these structural results pre-accepts it.

Harmony 2.4.1 source/IL inspection provides a narrower invariant. Its internal `HarmonySharedState.state` is exactly `Dictionary<MethodBase, byte[]>`; `UpdatePatchInfo(MethodBase, MethodInfo, PatchInfo)` serializes the new patch set to a new byte array and replaces the dictionary entry under the same dictionary lock after every patch/unpatch update. Candidate 3 retains the full `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` ownership validation only during activation, captures each validated entry's byte-array reference, and performs runtime integrity checks by locking the shared dictionary and comparing that reference. A changed, removed, foreign-owner, disposed-owner, or inaccessible stamp disables the same dependent capability; healing checks at each effect/health/buff callback boundary, while ordinary healing/combat/container coverage remains distributed across the existing two-second cycle. No accepted event, attribution, cleanup retry, or capability-degradation path is removed.

The candidate-3 structural gate passes 481/481 Release tests, including foreign patch token replacement, missing shared state, cross-patcher stamp rejection, and zero managed allocation across 10,000 trusted runtime stamp checks. The installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract probe verifies the exact internal shared-state field and update-method metadata without initializing Harmony under the probe's .NET 8 host. Frame-time analyzer and native Release builds pass with zero warnings/errors, the exact-five-file package validates, and compiled decompilation proves runtime stamp checks contain only dictionary locking/lookups/reference comparison while `IsPatchSetTrusted` remains confined to activation. Candidate 3 is structurally ready for native D repetition; these results do not pre-accept performance.

A final static audit after those captures found an activation-only time-of-check/time-of-use gap: candidate 3 fully validated each Harmony patch set and then captured its current shared-state token, so a foreign patch arriving in that narrow interval could have supplied a new token that had not itself been validated. The corrected sequence captures the token first, validates the complete owned/foreign patch metadata, and rechecks that the exact token is still current before publishing support. A faithful forced-interleaving regression snapshots safe metadata, injects a foreign prefix before the final token check, and proves activation rejects the changed token. The runtime stamp-check method and its allocation-free dictionary/reference path are unchanged. The corrected worktree passes 482/482 Release tests, a warning-free native build, formatter verification excluding only the two preserved line-ending-only user files, `git diff --check`, the installed-game contract probe, and IL inspection showing zero ordinary-production diagnostic boundary invocations. Candidate 3 remains valid diagnostic/performance evidence for the unchanged runtime approach, but its deployed bytes are superseded for immutable final-binary acceptance. Configuration B can proceed because UDS is disabled; configuration D and final primary acceptance must use the subsequently packaged and explicitly approved corrected production build.

Static tracing found one synchronous full-profile atomic save in `ProfileRepository.Record(ItemUseRecorded)` and another in `ProfileRepository.Record(HealingApplied)`, plus one synchronous diagnostic-document save for each success. The active profile was 1,241,588 bytes; a normal consumable therefore performed one full main-thread serialization/flush/replace, while each Med-Kit performed two in the same completion fan-out. The corrective worktree coalesces item-use plus healing mutations into one immutable frame-level profile snapshot, moves full serialization/durable replacement to a single-flight writer, drains and retries at every lifecycle/export/shutdown boundary, and removes only successful per-event diagnostic persistence. A persisted per-run lifetime watermark lets crash recovery apply exactly the aggregate delta present in the active checkpoint but absent from the latest profile snapshot; legacy immediate-save profiles remain unchanged. The expanded Release suite passes 496/496 and focused persistence/performance coverage passes 219/219, including explicit dirty-state retention after both a failed bounded write retry and a snapshot-capture failure plus an on-disk schema-5 analyzer regression proving fractional observed signal offsets override nominal requested offsets. The deferred-recovery regression also carries the exact recovered lifetime and run values through serialized JSON and every item-bearing CSV (`overview`, `groups`, `items`, starting-map totals, route-map totals, and segments), proving the UI/export source is not a narrower in-memory-only result. The native Release build succeeds with zero warnings/errors, formatter verification for the touched C# files passes, and `git diff --check` passes. Decompilation of candidate 5 confirms zero `NativeHotPathDiagnostics` invocations in every production caller type; the sole assembly-level call is the helper's internal `WriteSummary`-to-`Snapshot` implementation and is unreachable without a conditionally omitted caller. A new package/deployment and matched native consumable check remain required; none of these structural results is presented as native acceptance.

The matched consumable follow-up is frozen before its first capture as Harmony-only B versus hardened production D, three valid runs per configuration, in the same safe Nullpunkt area. The totem is unequipped in-raid for both B and D because its independent cadence could overlap the target completion and would exercise UDS-only health callbacks in D; all other equipment remains fixed.

The first completed Harmony-only use is retained as timing-calibration evidence only: it restored exactly 12 HP from 6.8/65 to 18.8/65, consumed one use, had no unrelated input or perceived hitch, and its broad nominal 5-15-second window contained no frame above 16.7 ms. The 7,065-frame record has a 4.173 ms median, 4.938 ms p99, and one isolated, unperceived 438.574 ms CPU-side pre-window stall at 4.632 seconds. Review then found that the controller sidecar stored requested offsets while four 250 ms audible signals and CapFrameX API latency made the true signal onsets later. This objective instrumentation defect—not the favorable result—prevents the pilot from serving as one of three matched runs. Schema-5 sidecars separately persist requested offsets, API-response latency, and request-relative observed signal offsets; the analyzer consumes the latter. The frozen method therefore requires three corrected B and three corrected D captures; the completed corrected B control is documented below, while D remains pending. D acceptance additionally requires exactly one item activation and the exact actual heal for each accepted use, with no duplicate attribution, persistence error, or lost event. Whole-record and completion-window frame distributions plus the exact completion-aligned peak are retained; a whole-record aggregate cannot dilute or replace the completion-frame comparison.

Candidate 5 is superseded before any D Med-Kit capture. A final lifecycle audit after the user's R1 readiness report found that `EnableDeferredItemPersistence` configured only the profile open during startup. A later save-slot open, user reset, save deletion, or new-game generation rotation could therefore replace `ProfileRepository.Current` with a profile lacking the deferred watermark root; the next consumable would throw before lifetime persistence. The user had neither pressed F11 nor used the Med-Kit, immediately extracted, and closed Duckov, so no candidate-5 D record or item event is accepted. The correction makes deferred item persistence a repository-lifetime mode, installs its root after every subsequent open/recovery, initializes every newly created generation in that mode, and makes repeated enablement idempotent without clearing an active watermark. A production-linked repository regression opens an existing pre-feature slot, enables the mode on another slot, reopens the existing slot, records an event, repeats enablement, rotates the generation, records again, and verifies the exact persisted watermark. The complete Release suite now passes 497/497.

Candidate 6 is superseded before any D Med-Kit capture or consumable use. The completed pre-native audit found five remaining ordering/composition cases: a delayed healing outcome from a completed run had to drain the deferred writer before its immediate profile save; a lifetime watermark could not be submitted until the active-run checkpoint containing the same mutation was durable; current-schema active-run item roots had to be validated before normalization so a malformed primary loses to an intact backup; same-frame projectile callbacks still had to compare the exact current generation/run instead of trusting only the frame cache; and item aggregate merging/recovery could not downgrade an item whose prior actual healing already proved the canonical Healing group. Combat capability fan-out now also attempts profile and active-run restriction independently. Deterministic failure, interleaving, recovery, slot/generation, identity, and composition regressions cover these cases. The complete Release suite passes 504/504. Candidate 6 remains installed only because Duckov is closed; it is not permitted D evidence and must be replaced by the newly frozen package before gameplay resumes.

The matched candidate-7 `D/consumable-medkit` cell is complete with three valid uses. The healing totem remained unequipped; the user declared a stable safe Nullpunkt setup with no active enemies, damage effect, passive healing, or unrelated input. Each action restored exactly 12 HP and advanced the same Med-Kit by one use: 9.3 to 21.3, 21.3 to 33.3, and 33.3 to 45.3. Persisted active-run, lifetime-profile, and deferred-watermark evidence agrees on exactly three activations, 75 durability consumed, 36 actual HP restored, and one item/healing event pair per use, with no UDS error. No hitch was perceived in any run. Runs 1-3 contain 6,665, 6,448, and 6,715 frames; whole-capture median/p99 values are 4.432/5.503, 4.484/5.512, and 4.342/5.684 ms, while observed-signal action windows measure 4.464/5.537, 4.505/5.673, and 4.385/5.671 ms. Exact completion-window maxima are 17.516, 6.347, and 6.157 ms. The aggregate is 4.432 ms whole median, 5.512 ms whole p99, 4.464 ms action median, and 5.671 ms action p99. Relative to B this is +5.498%/+9.677% whole and +5.868%/+11.191% action overhead. The whole median misses the 5% engineering target by 0.498 percentage points and the action median/p99 also miss the tighter target, but every measure passes the predeclared 10% median/20% p99 hard ceiling and there is no repeatable new action cluster.

The original completion regression is corrected: candidate 4 correlated all 8/8 Med-Kit completions with perceived 126.12-735.90 ms CPU stalls, while candidate 7's three exact completion windows peak at only 6.157-17.516 ms and none was perceived. D R2 and R3 nevertheless preserve isolated 691.783 and 388.423 ms CPU-side application frames 4.296 and 3.531 seconds after exact completion. They were not dropped presents, GPU busy was only 2.737/2.704 ms, neither formed a cluster, and the user perceived neither the use hitch nor a later freeze in R3; the separate later-freeze question was not answered for R2. B likewise has an isolated severe frame in R2 and R3, 5.058 and 3.214 seconds after inferred completion, with no perceived hitch or cluster. The shared 2-of-3 occurrence and delayed timing shape mean the larger D magnitudes remain unresolved rather than attributable to UDS from this six-run sample.

An offline scale check called candidate 7's exact `AtomicJsonStore<ProfileDocument>.Save` implementation 20 times after two warmups for repository-frozen 248,170, 467,324, 1,241,391, and 1,403,585-byte profiles. On PowerShell 7.6.3 / .NET 10.0.9 the per-case maxima were 9.972, 12.535, 28.467, and 15.867 ms with zero measured garbage collections. This cannot model Unity/Mono scheduling or contention and is not an acceptance benchmark, but it provides no supporting evidence that serialized profile size alone explains the delayed 59-692 ms frames. The exact matched timing, gate calculation, hashes, save-scale method, and conservative decision are frozen in `artifacts/performance/analysis/consumable-medkit-final-correlation.json` at SHA-256 `f6c73ab1af4d7124f41f42fb3c69fc1f66e65cfc514227631a258df9f0dc0e4b`.

After the three D uses, the user extracted and closed Duckov normally. The completed run remained eligible with all three item/healing pairs, all hooks unsubscribed in one cleanup sequence, no UDS error, no active-run/session residue, and a byte-identical primary/backup 159,115-byte profile. This closes the native Med-Kit correctness and clean-shutdown portion of the gate. The next performance decision point is a representative post-hardening Nullpunkt → Lagerbereich → Farmstadt run with an unlimited CapFrameX capture; another code change is not justified from the isolated maxima alone. Persistence UI/JSON/CSV projection and final package/deployment verification remain separate release gates.

The required repository audit before that soak found a separate multi-map correctness defect in candidate 7. The optimized equipment adapter suppressed an unchanged immutable loadout solely by snapshot identity, so a map transition with no equipment mutation did not publish that loadout into the new run segment. Event associations could still carry its stable identity, but the new segment could lack the local equipment-duration root required to resolve and aggregate those associations. The correction keys suppression by both the current run-segment identity and snapshot identity: the unchanged loadout is published exactly once after every segment change, while repeated same-segment firing/projectile/health associations retain the cached constant-time path. A production-linked regression proves the transition publication and stable association identity. The corrected source passes 2/2 focused adapter tests and the complete 505/505 Release suite, the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract probe, analyzer and native Release builds with zero warnings/errors, exact-five-file package validation, changed-file formatting, `git diff --check`, and tracked-binary safety. Decompilation of the changed production adapter confirms that compile-time diagnostic counters remain absent. Candidate 7's accepted single-segment Med-Kit evidence is not invalidated because it never crossed a segment boundary and the item/healing hot path is unchanged, but candidate 7 is superseded for final multi-map correctness, deployment, soak, and immutable-binary acceptance.

Candidate 8 is the immutable segment-aware package built from commit `fb61cd86c4a31ed786f50e87bb8d6c90bba5e94d` and frozen at `artifacts/performance/validation/candidate8-segment-cache-fb61cd8/UltimateDuckovStatistics`. Its exact files are `info.ini` (299 bytes, SHA-256 `189bbd81cb91434bd9908761409e7dd5da86cc9804bed2a03efce14c0871b357`), `INSTALL.md` (11,242 bytes, `37d834a1039b8b9466f8d6dcec84e15e9fafb00f156f2b4747a467f07944fe92`), `LICENSE` (1,117 bytes, `0f7558f2469ad0901074f6c380ada1ed91861d55adf905267bc70b26cd2e3ccc`), Core (358,912 bytes, `5506145a45ebc0b7acd98cd73538da4f0e48d80f22d0eddd573084586f313f12`), and native (218,112 bytes, `cf2e4f331d67865c1710d3a5ba0d977acfe277e184d91e30b3cd0e0781533823`). Both DLLs report file version `0.8.1.0` and product version `0.8.1+fb61cd86c4a31ed786f50e87bb8d6c90bba5e94d`; the sorted name/length/content inventory digest is `8a814b7b4aef1af0d7db0d4b823a6361597ea73e63e12430231d18f708cbee65`.

With fresh immediate user approval and Duckov process count zero, the candidate-8 transaction validated the frozen source, staging directory, and promoted installation. Independent readback found exactly the same five names, lengths, hashes, and DLL versions; no staging/previous residue remains and Duckov process count remains zero. The retained rollback baseline is `artifacts/performance/validation/pre-deployment-backup-candidate8-fb61cd8`: its 5 installed-mod files (589,170 bytes), all 500 UDS data files (16,922,155 bytes), and all 45 Duckov save files (23,205,391 bytes) were byte-identical to their sources before deployment. Post-deployment comparison again found zero differences across every UDS data and save relative path, length, and SHA-256. Candidate 8 is therefore the sole installed build permitted for the final representative soak.

### Post-hardening representative soak gate

This is a supplementary full-feature confidence run, not another matched D/B cell and not a replacement for the completed hard-ceiling matrix. It may begin only after the user explicitly says the stream is over and they are back; a Duckov process appearing is never that signal. Stream activity on save slot 2 remains outside this investigation. Before asking the user to set up the run, Codex must finish the repository/candidate audit, then—with the user back and Duckov closed—deploy and verify the segment-aware candidate-8 hashes and absence of deployment residue. The gameplay launch must use progressed slot 1 with exactly Harmony and UDS active. CapFrameX must use manual F11 capture, capture time `0` (unlimited), JSON output, zero delay, sensor logging off, and no effective RTSS overlay. Resolution remains 2560×1440 borderless fullscreen at 240 Hz, the in-game limiter remains 240 FPS, V-Sync and FSR remain off, and all other graphics settings remain at their highest available High/On values.

Start the capture at base immediately before entering the raid, then play a normal Nullpunkt → Lagerbereich → Farmstadt expedition rather than staging a stationary magazine test. Ordinary movement, combat, looting, healing, hydration, weapon swapping, and route loading are desired workload. The user records only noticeable hitches and their approximate map/action; no pauses for per-event messages are required. Extraction is preferred, but a normally finalized death after reaching Farmstadt remains analyzable. Stop the capture only after the terminal result or returned base is stable. Record starting/final inventory, MP7/MF durability and ammunition, outcome, route deviations, interruptions, unrelated input, and whether every Med-Kit or hydration completion felt smooth. An accidental short setup capture, premature F11 stop, save-slot mismatch, missing map, or unfinalized process termination is preserved but excluded rather than silently substituted.

After normal game closure, align the raw capture with the exact persisted run start/end and segment timestamps. The sidecar must contain the full raid, each map, both transitions, and equal-duration `raid-early`, `raid-middle`, and `raid-late` windows. The current analyzer was replayed offline against the original 15,030,682-byte candidate-4 capture and reproduced its 141,955 total frames, 139,455 raid frames, 4.571 ms raid median, 9.040 ms raid p99, and 10.391/9.464/8.650 ms early/middle/late p99 values through all nine named windows. Correlate every item/healing event to an exact ±0.25-second completion window and retain a broader two-seconds-before/three-seconds-after diagnostic window for delayed frames. Compare candidate 8 with candidate 4 descriptively only because natural combat is not a matched workload.

Reopen performance work only for evidence that survives this representative context: a perceived and event-aligned completion hitch, a rolling one-second cluster containing at least two frames above 33.3 ms, late-third p99 more than 20% worse than early-third p99, a repeated delayed pattern tied to the same UDS event/lifecycle boundary, or any missing/duplicate/misattributed/persistence error. Isolated unperceived frames remain visible but are not an automatic failure. Acceptance additionally requires exact run/profile/UI/JSON/CSV agreement, one clean setup/cleanup sequence, no UDS error, normal checkpoint removal, and byte-identical primary/backup state after shutdown.

The first candidate-8 soak attempt is retained but excluded from the full-route gate because a normal death on Lagerbereich ended the planned Nullpunkt → Lagerbereich → Farmstadt route before Farmstadt and the second transition. The unlimited CapFrameX capture contains 86,065 raw frames across 391.835 seconds; SHA-256 is `95d4cd56fd368ea1053082dcc9693b8ab0846abaf0f7b60e8965d68813a3a641`. Persisted run `b421d7627f6f469b8f905098d4ed5ef4` finalized as an eligible death with 362.233 active seconds, 246 firing actions, 245 completed projectiles, 166 ranged hits, 20 kills, six unique containers, three Med-Kit (S) uses, one Lolli use, and one Kartoffel use. Nullpunkt records 159.423 active seconds and Lagerbereich 202.811. RES III, and Totem: Heilung III loadout, directly proving candidate 8 republishes an unchanged snapshot after the segment change.

Equal early/middle/late raid slices measure 7.723/11.234/6.752 ms p99; the middle slice contains the transition, while late p99 is 12.575% better than early p99. ### Baseline and isolation gate

1. Use the review-hardened merged M8 build and confirm exactly one weapon, combat, equipment, and run-lifecycle subscription set.
2. In a comparable scene, perform a cold-launch idle baseline plus sustained empty-space and enemy-hit firing with a representative high-rate automatic weapon, a second automatic weapon with a different class or effect profile, and a slower-firing control. Keep each paired enabled/disabled weapon, ammunition, and loadout configuration identical.
3. Compare UDS enabled and disabled. Repeat each configuration/scenario at least three times when making a quantitative acceptance claim.
4. Capture frame times; averaged FPS alone is insufficient. Record the exact UDS build, enabled adapters, save generation, route/association size, diagnostics state, graphics settings, and capture conditions.
5. Isolate subsystem combinations with profiling builds or other controlled means. Historical release comparison may help locate the introduction window, but the final acceptance build must run the complete production feature set.

### Automated and structural gate

- Instrument the checkpoint store and deep-copy boundary with deterministic counters. High-rate shot and hit bursts within one checkpoint interval must perform in-memory reduction without per-event serialization, flush, atomic replacement, or full-route clone.
- Exercise a growing multi-segment run through the 2,048-association bound. Saturation must preserve prior evidence and overall M1-M8 totals while degrading only the documented route-dependent capabilities.
- Cover successful coalescing, dirty-state preservation, failed-write retry, mutations immediately before, during, and after a scheduled snapshot, transition flush, extraction, death, interruption, clean shutdown, abrupt termination, and exactly-once recovery. If the implementation introduces background work, cover its synchronization and disposal explicitly.
- Verify firing, projectile, health, equipment-association, and route fan-out counts remain identical before and after the optimization. No event may be silently sampled, dropped, merged, duplicated, attributed to the wrong segment, or recaptured from later equipment state.
- Inspect measured callback paths for synchronous durable I/O, growing aggregate clones, unbounded allocation, reflection, metadata/display-name lookup, capability publication, logging, or UI work. Optimize only costs demonstrated by profiling or structural evidence.
- Run the complete Release suite, installed-game contract probe, warning-free native Release build, formatter/diff checks, source-safety audit, package validation, and independent package extraction.

Wall-clock performance assertions do not belong in ordinary CI because shared-runner timing is unstable. CI proves bounded work and call frequency structurally; the user-controlled native run proves actual frame-time behavior.

### Native performance matrix

Codex prepares and deploys the compared builds while Duckov is closed, verifies hashes and activation state, and inspects UDS logs/profile/export evidence. Only the user launches and controls Duckov.

For each paired enabled/disabled configuration, use the same save, map area, graphics settings, resolution, frame limiter/v-sync state, weapon, ammunition, equipment, enemy conditions where practical, and capture duration. Record at least three runs of:

1. Idle/no firing.
2. Sustained firing into empty space with a representative high-rate automatic weapon.
3. Sustained firing with the same weapon while hitting one enemy.
4. Sustained firing with the same weapon into multiple targets where safely reproducible.
5. Repeat the empty-space and single-enemy controls with a second automatic weapon from a different weapon class or effect profile.
6. Repeat the relevant controls with a slower-firing weapon.

Use a frame-time capture tool and retain its raw machine-specific artifacts outside Git when large. Report median, p95, p99, maximum frame time, frames above 16.7 ms, and frames above 33.3 ms. Unless an explicit product decision records a different budget before optimization, the median of three UDS-enabled captures must be no more than 10% worse than disabled at median frame time, no more than 20% worse at p99, and must introduce no repeatable new cluster of frames above 33.3 ms.

After the enabled benchmark, finish or interrupt the run through the scenario selected before launch, export once, and close normally. Profile, active/recovered checkpoint where applicable, UI, JSON, and all CSV projections must agree on every action and outcome. Logs must show one setup/cleanup sequence, bounded checkpoint writes rather than one write per shot/hit, no duplicate subscriptions, and no UDS exception. Performance is not accepted by disabling metrics, weakening attribution, silently losing events, or reducing crash-safety without an explicit product decision.

## M9 v0.9.0 Economy candidate

### Installed native contract

Discovery was performed read-only against Duckov `2.3.30`, Steam build `24013657`, Unity `2022.3.62f2`, and Harmony `2.4.1.0`. `TeamSoda.Duckov.Core.dll` is SHA-256 `298d5d5885427632d5a94b2f3ce587f8ebc9528ec71e575a475158c326ecae8f`; `ItemStatsSystem.dll` is `a276e15c022f71b2214bd05e1b9b0f2e620c16561df576fed0b79c2fe4402e60`; installed `0Harmony.dll` is `353daafec180bb8e7bbe4da78f2a7cdc78067392e3a4e79dc8e7af295f2371e6`. M9 adds no Harmony owner.

The verified public contract is:

- `Duckov.Economy.EconomyManager.Money` is a `long`. Its private setter captures the old value, mutates the field, then invokes `OnMoneyChanged(old,new)`. `SetupSaveData` assigns the backing field directly, so profile load does not emit a Money flow. The callback proves exact post-mutation amount and direction, but not a semantic reason.
- `EconomyManager.Pay(Cost)` can make component mutations before complete cost success; only `OnCostPaid(Cost)` is after the full cost completes. That event does not identify purchase versus crafting versus another cost, so it never upgrades a Money delta to `Purchase` or `FeeOrCraftingCost`.
- `StockShop.OnItemSoldByPlayer(shop,item,sellPrice)` occurs after a completed sale. A same-boundary exact matching Money delta is `Sale`; otherwise the exact physical-Cash delta is `Sale`. The public purchase callback lacks the final charged amount and occurs on an asynchronous path, so purchase-source attribution remains unknown.
- `Duckov.Quests.Reward.OnRewardClaimed(Reward)` occurs after claim completion. A `QuestReward_Money.Amount` matching the exact pending Money delta is `Reward`. Non-Money rewards do not create Money flows.
- Physical Cash is stackable item type `EconomyManager.CashItemID == 451`; `EconomyManager.Cash` returns the owned item count as `long`. M9 reads `ItemUtilities.FindAllBelongsToPlayer` only after native inventory/economy boundaries and totals storage, exact-main inventory, and pet inventory ownership. The native helper concatenates those ownership roots without identity deduplication, so one reconciliation deduplicates equal runtime item identities and rejects conflicting same-scan counts before summing. `OnPlayerItemOperation`, main-inventory, storage, and pet callbacks dirty one coalesced reconciliation; ordinary clean frames do no wallet read or inventory scan. `LevelManager.OnLevelBeginInitializing` first drains any dirty exact Cash delta under its captured pre-transition context, then suspends reconciliation while ownership roots hydrate; `OnAfterLevelInitialized` establishes the complete loaded inventory as a non-economic baseline.
- `InteractablePickup.OnPickupSuccess(pickup,character)` is after successful pickup and after production `Inventory.AddAndMerge`. M9 requires the exact main character, an active run, Cash type 451, and a matching positive owned-total delta. It records event-time run/map/segment/context/integrity. Removed Cash item identities and their last exact owned amounts are retained only in a bounded 512-entry runtime map. This prevents same-session drop/re-pickup from becoming a second acquisition even when `AddAndMerge` consumes the picked item to stack count zero and only a different compatible stack survives the post-pickup snapshot; runtime object identity is never persisted as currency identity.
- Public extraction/death callbacks do not prove acquired-unit disposition across fungible main, pet, and storage Cash ownership. `native-economy-cash-terminal` is therefore `DisabledIncompatible`. Run completion preserves acquired totals and writes them to `Unresolved`; it does not claim secured or lost amounts.

The adapter contract version is `native-economy/2.3.30+public-events-v3`. Money amount/direction, Cash amount/direction, Cash context, and route attribution are `Supported`; Money source/context and Cash external acquisition are `Experimental`; Cash terminal outcome is `DisabledIncompatible`. The installed-game contract probe verifies each event/property/type above, including `OnLevelBeginInitializing`, and the fixed game/build/assembly baseline before a native build can pass.
