## M9 scalable economy idempotency correction

The earlier v0.9.0 candidate's 2,048-entry `RecentEventIds` hard stop is superseded and is not an accepted delivery contract. That build stopped every otherwise-supported economy aggregate after its identity list filled, disabled Money, Cash, and route economy capabilities, and could leave lifetime, active-run, segment, starting-map, and route-map totals permanently incomplete at normal transaction volume. Its implementation commit, package identity, deployment proof, cold-launch proof, 589-test count, and draft-delivery claim are retained only as superseded evidence. The separate M8 limit of 2,048 `SegmentEventAssociation` rows per run remains out of scope; it may disable general event-association evidence, but it must not stop economy reduction or otherwise-supported economy map totals.

The production lifecycle trace establishes the following ordering and replay boundaries. `NativeEconomyAdapter` creates each normalized flow synchronously under one random activation ID and a strictly increasing positive sequence. It then attempts the profile and active-run destinations independently, in that order, before another adapter event ID is created. `NativeProfileCoordinator` mutates the in-memory lifetime profile and marks its single-flight profile writer dirty. Active-raid flows independently mutate the active run and event-time segment, dirtying the coalesced checkpoint. An ordinary profile write is allowed only after the current active-run checkpoint revision is durable; a failed checkpoint blocks the profile write, and failed profile writes retain their dirty revision for retry. The deferred profile state stores the exact active-run economy already applied to lifetime. After abrupt termination, recovery selects the first structurally and semantically valid active-checkpoint primary, backup, or temporary candidate, subtracts that durable lifetime watermark from checkpoint totals, merges only the non-negative exact difference, finalizes the interrupted run once, durably saves the profile, and then deletes all three checkpoint candidates. If the run ID already exists, the stale finalized checkpoint is cleared without applying either lifetime or completed-run totals again. Completed-run, starting-map, and route-map fan-out is guarded by the persisted run ID before any aggregate merge. Save-generation validation prevents an archived or rotated generation's flow or checkpoint from entering the new generation.

The corrected design uses an activation-scoped monotonic replay cursor on each directly recording economy aggregate. The cursor stores only the current producer activation ID and the greatest sequence whose delivery window is closed for that aggregate. Registration persists the new activation with closed-through sequence zero before its first positive-sequence event; that state is valid and must survive cloning, persistence, and restart without being labelled repaired. Accepting sequence `N` closes every lower sequence for that aggregate: because ID creation and destination publication are synchronous and ordered, a lower sequence not previously delivered to that aggregate cannot become a valid first delivery later. It is therefore either a duplicate/stale replay or violates the verified adapter ordering contract and is rejected. Sequence gaps are supported without storing them; base-only events, other runs, and earlier segments can legitimately consume those numbers before a particular run or segment first observes the activation. A different activation cannot enter a non-empty active aggregate. At profile open, interrupted recovery runs before a newly created adapter activation is registered. Registration preserves the cursor when the same activation reopens a slot in-process and replaces a dead activation only after its surviving checkpoint primary, backup, and temporary artifacts have been consumed, deleted, or archived. This provides exact replay rejection with constant metadata per aggregate rather than one persisted identity per transaction.

Current schema-9 candidate compatibility remains in schema 9. Unsaturated legacy `RecentEventIds` are validated and retained through backup/checkpoint candidate selection, then removed only at the post-recovery safe boundary above. A legacy `DeduplicationSaturated` marker preserves all recorded exact totals, becomes an explicit legacy-incomplete-history marker, and no longer disables future capture after the dead activation's recovery artifacts are gone. Valid old candidates may omit the new cursor. New candidates with a present but malformed activation/sequence cursor are rejected before normalization so a valid backup or temporary candidate can win. Normalization is idempotent. Replay cursors do not participate in completed-run aggregate merging or checkpoint-minus-profile arithmetic: those boundaries use run identity and exact aggregate composition, respectively. Money and Cash arithmetic overflow remains independent and continues to retain prior exact values while disabling only the unrepresentable currency.

Required corrective validation includes more than 2,048 Money events, more than 2,048 Cash events, a mixed stream, a 100,000-event lifetime stress, fixed-size replay metadata, stable serialized profile size apart from counter/revision digits and legitimate breakdowns, duplicate delivery on both sides of checkpoint/profile durability, write failure/retry, newer-checkpoint and newer-profile recovery, repeated recovery, finalized and interrupted checkpoint windows, multiple activations, save rotation, sequence gaps and stale out-of-order rejection, old unsaturated and saturated schema-9 candidates, malformed primary/backup/temporary selection, exact run/segment/starting-map/route-map composition independent of the remaining route-association ceiling, projection agreement, independent arithmetic overflow, no per-event durable profile write, O(1) clean economy tick, and no M1-M8.1 regression.

The correction passes 606/606 tests in both development and complete Release runs. `OneHundredThousandLifetimeEventsKeepConstantReplayMetadata` and `SerializedProfileSizeDoesNotGrowWithHistoricalEconomyIdentities` prove exact 50,000/50,000 Money/Cash composition, an empty historical ID list, one activation/sequence cursor, and no serialized growth proportional to transaction count. Separate 4,096-event Money and Cash regressions retain supported capture. A 5,000-event two-segment run continues exact run, segment, and starting-map totals after the independent route-association ceiling disables route-map evidence for that run; 2,500 one-event runs separately prove starting-map and route-map economy aggregates beyond the legacy identity limit without approaching the per-run association bound. Deferred-profile/checkpoint tests reject duplicates on both sides of durability, recover only checkpoint-minus-watermark totals, repeat recovery without reapplication, retry a failed bounded profile snapshot, and prove legacy evidence is not compacted until the checkpoint has been recovered and its primary/backup/temporary artifacts are deleted. Schema-9 tests preserve unsaturated and old-saturated totals, mark the latter explicitly incomplete, resume under a new activation, and choose valid backup/temporary candidates over malformed replay cursors in both profile and active-run stores. The installed Duckov contract probe, frame-time analyzer, warning-free Core/native Release builds, formatter, source-safety audit, and exact five-file package-directory validation pass.

### M9 post-sequence-zero review corrections

The sequence-zero package and minimal cold persistence check above remain valid historical evidence for that boundary, but later review supersedes ZIP SHA-256 `d2fa07eb39c2edb46bf0c42a2bf763798f2913751b75bb9742446ab4cff5b217` as the final M9 candidate. Three defects were confirmed. First, production `Inventory.AddAndMerge` can consume a dropped Cash item into a different compatible stack before `OnPickupSuccess`; the old reconciliation then found no picked runtime ID in the owned snapshot and fabricated the full increase as a new external acquisition. Second, `OnLevelBeginInitializing` reset a dirty Cash baseline without reconciling it, so a corpse/container transfer immediately before transition could be lost. Third, checkpoint-minus-watermark recovery treated a saturation-only difference as empty, so a crash after durable checkpoint saturation but before profile durability could reopen lifetime Money as supported.

Implementation commit `20fa8acdc873e331515d9155ed838b96cb820b7a` retains each removed player-originated Cash identity with its last exact owned amount; merged re-pickups therefore remain exact `UnknownAdjustment` inflows with `ProvenExternalRaidAcquisition=false` even when the picked item is absent and has stack count zero after the production merge. Level initialization now flushes dirty Cash under the captured pre-transition run/map/segment context before baseline suspension and hydration. Deferred economy recovery treats a newly saturated Money or Cash flag as a recoverable checkpoint delta even when all counters equal the persisted watermark, merges the narrow capability degradation once, retains the prior exact `Int64` maximum, and remains idempotent after clean close.

`DropRepickupConsumedByAddAndMergeIsNotAcquiredAgain` models the production merge order with a different surviving stack and a consumed picked item. `SceneInitializationFlushesPendingCashBeforeSuspendingTheBaseline` enters initialization before the next coalesced tick and proves the exact old-context delta is emitted once. `DeferredArithmeticSaturationRecoversFromCheckpointWhenProfileStillHasTheExactMaximum` persists an unsaturated `Int64.MaxValue` profile/watermark, durably writes a newer saturated checkpoint, reopens without a profile save, and proves lifetime plus completed-run degradation and repeated-reopen idempotency. The focused 20-test gate and complete Debug/Release suites pass; the new total is 609/609 in each configuration. ### M9 lifetime capability and new-raid terminal corrections

A later review supersedes the `d5a3aba` artifact as the final candidate while retaining its accepted live evidence. Three defects were confirmed: a successful adapter initialization replaced persisted lifetime degradation with Supported after restart; zero-flow degraded completed runs were mistaken for uninitialized aggregates and could be upgraded by a later supported flow; and the different-native-raid interruption fallback completed the old run without first draining its queued economy observer.

Implementation `19c80465f3b420e8454e085bd55a02019b0e5588` distinguishes the blank default capability set from real zero-flow capability evidence. Lifetime publication initializes only a genuinely blank aggregate and thereafter restricts availability monotonically, so a persisted runtime discontinuity or Cash scan failure cannot be erased by restart. Aggregate fan-out uses the same distinction, preserving a degraded zero-flow run across completed-run, starting-map, and route-map totals while retaining exact later counters. A production-linked `NativeRunTerminalBoundary` owns `SetTerminalObserver`; both ordinary terminal handling and `OnNewRaid` pass through it before tracker completion, so captured Money and dirty Cash publish under the old run identity.

`RestartedSupportedAdapterCannotErasePersistedLifetimeCapabilityLoss` degrades Cash capture, closes, reopens, publishes a supported adapter snapshot, and verifies both live and reloaded lifetime capability remain unavailable with the original reason. `ZeroFlowDegradedAggregateRemainsDegradedAfterSupportedFlowMerge` covers the reducer boundary, while `ZeroFlowDegradedRunKeepsCompletedAndMapTotalsDegradedAfterLaterSupportedFlow` exercises complete run fan-out and strict composition validation. `NewRaidDrainsQueuedEconomyBeforeInterruptingTheOldRun` drives the linked production terminal boundary with a queued Money flow and proves the interrupted old run contains it.

### M9 lifetime-scope UI wording correction

A corrective-diff review found one direct UI regression after the lifetime-capability fix. Retaining a disabled lifetime capability after a transient runtime failure and successful restart is correct, but compact Economy text derived its `current capture unavailable` annotation solely from that scope-level capability. It therefore contradicted the separately supported live adapter and diagnostic rows.

Implementation `8aaaacd9533182feb5ffaa8ec3d2889773420f41` exposes a defensive clone of the coordinator's current economy capabilities to the panel. Compact lifetime, completed-run, recent-run, and nonzero Cash-outcome projections compare each scope capability with that live snapshot. A disabled live metric still renders `current capture unavailable`; when the live metric is Supported or Experimental but the retained scope is degraded, the UI instead renders `capture unavailable for part of this scope`. A formatter without a live snapshot uses the scope-neutral `capture unavailable for this scope`. `EconomyUiProjectionDistinguishesDegradedLifetimeFromCurrentCaptureFailure` covers the supported-current/degraded-lifetime split for both Money totals and Cash acquisition outcomes and rejects the false current-unavailability wording.

### M9 detailed Economy scope wording correction

Review after the compact projection correction found that `NativeStatisticsPanel.DrawEconomy` captured the current `EconomyMetricCapabilities` but did not carry the matching live amount capability into `DrawCurrency` or the four detailed Cash raid-outcome rows. Those ten values instead used panel-local `FormatLong`, which knew only the persisted scope state and labeled every nonzero disabled-scope value as `current capture unavailable`. This could contradict a healthy current adapter after a transient historical failure without affecting compact Overview, Runs, or recent-run text.

Implementation `5e27359357f4433de11be329cd2cc7a785b2687c` removes the divergent panel formatter. Money and Cash gross inflow, gross outflow, and net flow now receive the matching current amount capability; acquired receives current Cash-acquisition availability; and secured, lost, and unresolved receive current Cash-terminal availability. Detailed values and compact Cash outcomes call the shared testable `UiText.FormatEconomyValue`; compact totals and that formatter use the same `FormatUnavailableScope` wording decision. Nonzero disabled scope plus Supported or Experimental current capture renders `capture unavailable for part of this scope`; disabled current capture renders `current capture unavailable`; omitting the live snapshot renders `capture unavailable for this scope`; and zero unavailable values remain `Unsupported`. No aggregate, persistence, recovery, migration, adapter, JSON, or CSV semantics changed.

`DetailedEconomyValueFormatterDistinguishesScopeFromCurrentCaptureFailure` invokes the exact formatter used by the detailed production rows. It covers nonzero degraded Money and Cash totals with Supported current capture, degraded Cash acquisition with Experimental current capture, degraded Cash terminal outcome with DisabledIncompatible current capture, the no-live-snapshot fallback, and zero acquisition/terminal values remaining Unsupported. The pre-existing `EconomyUiProjectionDistinguishesDegradedLifetimeFromCurrentCaptureFailure` remains green. ### M9 terminal economy checkpoint correction

A recovery review found that the pre-terminal observer could flush queued Money or dirty Cash into the active tracker after its most recent checkpoint, while terminal handling only drained an older pending write before clearing the tracker. If completed-run persistence then failed or the process crashed, the recovery artifact could omit the terminal mutation even though the lifetime snapshot retained it. The new-raid interruption fallback shared the same exposure.

Implementation `af3be5ad5946b05cc9227d979a2243e41445dbd2` gives `NativeRunTerminalBoundary.Apply` an explicit pre-completion checkpoint seam. Ordinary extraction, death, and interruption handling now runs the economy observer, final movement sample, and integrity observation, then creates and awaits the refreshed active-run checkpoint before applying the terminal event. The different-native-raid fallback uses the same boundary and checkpoint ordering. `TerminalBoundaryCheckpointsQueuedEconomyBeforeTheRunBecomesInactive` starts with an older durable checkpoint, queues a +17 Money mutation in the exact production observer order, writes the refreshed checkpoint at the production seam, terminalizes without completing the repository run, and simulates a crash/reopen. Recovery retains +17 exactly in lifetime, run, segment, completed-run, starting-map, and route-map economy.

The production-linked boundary tests pass 2/2, and the complete Debug and Release suites pass 632/632. `scripts/build.ps1` passes the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract probe, frame-time analyzer, warning-free Core/native Release builds, and exact package-directory validation. Whole-solution `dotnet format --verify-no-changes`, `git diff --check`, and the tracked forbidden-binary source-safety scan pass. No release ZIP was created, Duckov was not launched, saves/profiles and the installed mod were not modified, and no deployment, merge, tag, release, or publication occurred. Read-only GitHub verification on 2026-08-17 found draft PR #10 open and unmerged at pushed head `fb2cd7cbd4f3217ca3d798b73cea84eeff60e848`, with `core` and `source-safety` both passed; local correction `af3be5a` has not been pushed and therefore has no CI evidence. The protected untracked M8.1 report remains excluded.

### M9 terminal checkpoint durability-failure correction

Follow-up review proved that the successful-write ordering above was insufficient: `SaveCheckpoint` returned a durability result, but the boundary accepted an `Action` and terminalized even when submission or the awaited write failed. A successful lifetime snapshot followed by a crash could therefore recover the older checkpoint and omit the terminal flow from run, segment, starting-map, and route-map aggregates.

Implementation `17e57b169488b359c05cd66207c82307cfde9f97` changes the terminal seam to a Boolean durability contract. A failed checkpoint keeps the tracker active and retains the original terminal event, including its UTC and monotonic timestamps. Normal ticks retry only the checkpoint and do not invoke the economy observer or final state capture again; profile transitions fail closed while durability is unavailable; cleanup remains retryable; and the profile-writer checkpoint barrier uses the retained terminal timestamp so it cannot extend the run past the observed terminal boundary. Only a successful durable retry clears the pending event and calls `RunLifecycleTracker.Apply`.

`FailedTerminalCheckpointKeepsTheRunActiveUntilDurableRetry` injects a failed first attempt, proves the run remains active, verifies the exact event survives, then proves a successful retry terminalizes without a second economy observation. `TerminalBoundaryCheckpointsQueuedEconomyBeforeTheRunBecomesInactive` now starts with an older checkpoint, injects the failed terminal write, asserts the tracker remains active, durably retries, and simulates crash recovery across all six economy scopes. `ReadmeDelegatesMutablePullRequestStateInsteadOfEmbeddingAStaleSnapshot` prevents the active-candidate status paragraph from embedding commit-like identifiers and requires it to delegate live head, review, and CI state to PR #10; immutable evidence remains here in `TESTING.md`.

Focused failure/recovery/status coverage passes 4/4, and the complete Debug and Release suites pass 634/634. `scripts/build.ps1` passes the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract probe, frame-time analyzer, warning-free Core/native Release builds, and exact package-directory validation. Whole-solution formatting, `git diff --check`, and tracked forbidden-binary source safety pass. ### M9 same-process terminal-cleanup dependency correction

Corrective-diff review found that a failed terminal checkpoint during deactivation retained the retryable run-lifecycle adapter but allowed `ModBehaviour.Cleanup` to dispose its `NativeProfileCoordinator`. The retained adapter's checkpoint and completion delegates then targeted a coordinator whose repository had been cleared, so every same-process cleanup retry failed and replacement activation remained blocked until process restart.

Implementation `f258c3c039a53ca4e0da379ce5be960967864c09` extends `ProcessLifetimeCleanupOwner` with a completion retained only after an owned cleanup failure. `ModBehaviour.Cleanup` supplies disposal of the exact current profile coordinator as that completion. An immediately successful lifecycle cleanup preserves the existing end-of-cleanup coordinator disposal order. A failed lifecycle cleanup retains both objects; the old behaviour may finish disposing unrelated adapters, but the coordinator repository remains live. When either the same behaviour or a later process-lifetime owner successfully retries the lifecycle cleanup, the retained completion disposes the coordinator only after the adapter has durably checkpointed and terminalized the run.

`FailedProcessLifetimeCleanupKeepsItsDependencyAliveUntilSameProcessRetry` composes the production cleanup owner with a cleanup resource that fails once and requires its coordinator-like dependency on retry. It proves the dependency is not disposed after the first failure, the replacement process-lifetime owner can retry successfully, the dependency was available during that retry, and disposal occurs afterward. Focused cleanup coverage passes 3/3, and the complete Debug and Release suites pass 635/635. ### M9 completed-run persistence and upgraded-profile fan-out correction

Follow-up review found two residual recovery gaps. First, after the durable pre-terminal checkpoint made the tracker inactive, a failed completed-profile save discarded the in-memory terminal summary; a crash then recovered the checkpoint as Interrupted and lost the real outcome, record eligibility, and Cash terminal disposition. Second, schema-8 migration correctly retained `HistoricalUnavailable`, but that historical coverage marker caused newly captured exact M9 currency rows to bypass completed-run, starting-map, and route-map composition checks forever.

The terminal checkpoint now records the intended `Extracted`, `Died`, or `Interrupted` outcome before tracker completion. Recovery closes an open segment with that exact outcome, recomputes record eligibility under the normal lifecycle/integrity contract, and finalizes Cash terminal disposition consistently. A production-linked completion boundary retains the exact inactive summary until the coordinator reports durable success; normal ticks, profile transitions, and retryable cleanup all retry it. `ProfileRepository.CompleteRun` separately remembers an in-process failed save or checkpoint deletion so the already-reduced summary is durably saved and acknowledged on retry. Repeated native raid initialization with the same identity no longer invokes the terminal observer or writes a misleading terminal checkpoint.

Composition validation now distinguishes unavailable pre-M9 coverage from exact post-M9 rows. An upgraded aggregate with a captured, unsaturated currency row must equal the sum of current exact components while historical components with no such row contribute recorded zero. `FailedCompletedRunPersistenceRetainsTheExactSummaryForRetry`, `TerminalCheckpointRecoversExactOutcomeWhenCompletedRunSaveNeverLands`, and `RepeatedNativeRaidIdentityDoesNotCreateATerminalCheckpoint` cover the retry, crash, and identity boundaries. `MigratedProfileWithPostM9EconomyStillRejectsCrossScopeMismatch` proves completed-run, starting-map, and route-map corruption in a newer upgraded primary loses to the intact backup.

### M9 upgraded-profile missing-total-row correction

Further corrective review found that the upgraded-profile composition rule still began only when the historical aggregate already contained the currency row. Removing a completed-run, starting-map, or route-map Money row from a newer primary therefore disabled validation for that currency even when an exact post-M9 component retained a nonzero contribution. The corrupted primary could defeat an intact backup and expose zero in one scope while run and map scopes retained the exact flow.

Historical composition now begins whenever the total is historical and its currency arithmetic is unsaturated, regardless of whether its currency row exists. A missing total row compares as recorded zero against the exact sum of component rows, so it is rejected when any component contributes a nonzero amount. A genuinely all-zero historical composition remains valid; missing unsaturated current component rows remain exact zero even when capture capability degraded; and saturated totals or components still do not claim exact composition. The persistence regression now removes the Money row independently from completed-run, starting-map, and route-map totals and proves each newer primary loses to the intact backup, while the direct reducer regression covers both the removed nonzero total and valid all-zero boundary.

### M9 completed Cash spending identity-cache correction

Corrective-diff review found that every vanished Cash runtime identity entered the bounded player-originated drop/re-pickup cache before a completed cost reduced the amount considered dropped. Repeatedly spending distinct full Cash stacks could therefore exhaust the 512-entry cache and unnecessarily degrade external-acquisition capability. The explicit `Cost.items` amount protected only the outside-owned magnitude, not the retained identities, and did not cover physical Cash consumed for `Cost.money`.

Read-only inspection of the installed Duckov 2.3.30 implementation confirmed the exact public-event order: `Pay(Cost)` mutates Money first, consumes any remaining `Cost.money` through physical Cash, publishes `OnMoneyPaid`, consumes `Cost.items`, and finally publishes `OnCostPaid`. Economy adapter v5 now drains an earlier dirty Cash boundary from `OnMoneyChanged`, observes the physical `Cost.money` portion at `OnMoneyPaid`, and retains vanished identities only when the resulting exact outflow contains a player-originated amount. Completed `Cost.money` and explicit Cash `Cost.items` spending therefore never enters the cache, while a real drop preceding either cost remains retained for neutral re-pickup. The native contract probe now requires the public `OnMoneyPaid` event and `Cost.money` field.

`RepeatedCompletedCashSpendingDoesNotConsumeTheDropIdentityBound` consumes 513 distinct full stacks independently through explicit Cash items and physical `Cost.money`, proves acquisition remains Experimental, and then proves a genuine raid pickup is still recorded as external acquisition. `MoneyFundedCashCostFlushesAnEarlierDropBeforeConsumingCash` covers the verified callback boundary with a preceding drop, physical spending, AddAndMerge re-pickup, and neutral acquisition. The genuine-drop saturation regression remains green and still degrades before an eviction could double count.

### M9 coalesced Cash drop/cost identity correction

Follow-up review found that the preceding correction still retained every vanished identity whenever one player-originated unit remained in a coalesced outflow. A full-stack `Cost.items` payment combined with one real drop could therefore add both the dropped identity and all cost-destroyed identities, reaching the defensive bound immediately. The default `Pay(Cost)` path emits an `OnMoneyChanged` boundary before item consumption, including a zero Money assignment, but read-only inspection of every installed 2.3.30 call site found a real bypass: a cash-only `CostTaker` calls `cost.Pay(!cashOnly)` with `accountAvaliable: false` and can proceed directly to item consumption.

Duckov supplies exact per-identity evidence for that bypass. `ItemUtilities.ConsumeItems(Cost)` calls `DestroyTree()` for every fully consumed stack; `DestroyTree()` calls public `Item.MarkDestroyed()` before the deferred Unity destruction, so public `Item.IsBeingDestroyed` is true synchronously before `OnCostPaid`. Economy adapter v6 retains the prior owned `Item` reference alongside each amount. When an identity vanishes, a destroyed item is removed from any prior drop cache and excluded from the bound, while a still-live vanished item remains eligible drop/re-pickup evidence. This does not guess which fungible units funded the cost and does not evict a possible live drop. The installed contract probe now requires `Item.IsBeingDestroyed`.

`CoalescedFullStackCashCostRetainsOnlyTheLiveDroppedIdentity` starts with 514 distinct one-unit stacks, leaves one live drop, marks the other 513 exactly as native `DestroyTree()` does, and coalesces the complete `Cost.items` outflow without a Money boundary. It proves the capability remains Experimental, the live identity re-picks neutrally, and a later genuine raid pickup is still externally acquired. Pure spending, partial-stack overlap, AddAndMerge re-pickup, and genuine bound saturation remain green.

### M9 activation, Cash outcome, terminal retry, and invariant export correction

Further review found four independent durability and reporting gaps. A failed sequence-zero activation save left the new replay cursor only in memory and suppressed an identical retry; the resulting profile-change exception could also skip the Cash/Money baseline reset. Recovery composition checked currency flows but not the separately exported Cash raid outcomes. A deferred terminal checkpoint retried before the normal economy tick without draining events queued after the first attempt. Finally, source and context CSVs formatted signed `long` values through the process culture.

Activation registration is now transactional: a failed atomic save restores the prior economy aggregate, revision, and update timestamp, while the coordinator retains the activation for retry before the next gameplay tick. Profile-changed subscribers are published independently, so one failure cannot skip baseline reset. Cash acquisition is validated from segments into each run, and complete acquired/secured/lost/unresolved outcomes are validated from runs into completed-run and starting-map totals and from segments into route-map totals; inconsistent newer primaries lose to intact backups. Every terminal checkpoint retry invokes the terminal observer again before refreshing the checkpoint, and both detailed economy CSVs now format inflow, outflow, and net flow with invariant culture.

The forced-write-failure regression blocks the atomic `.tmp` path, proves complete in-memory rollback, removes the fault, retries the identical activation, and reads the durable sequence-zero cursor back. Cash outcome corruption is injected independently at run-segment, completed-run, starting-map, and route-map scopes. The terminal retry regression queues Money only after the failed checkpoint and proves the completed summary contains it. The export regression runs under `fa-IR` and requires invariant ASCII `-3` in both source and context CSVs. Independent profile-change publication is production-linked and proves a throwing activation subscriber does not skip the later reset subscriber.

### M9 durable-activation publication gate correction

Follow-up review confirmed that transactional sequence-zero rollback and subscriber isolation did not by themselves prevent publication while activation persistence remained unavailable. The coordinator retried before the normal update but exposed no readiness result, the update continued into the economy tick, synchronous Cash callbacks could flush independently, and the flow handler did not reject a still-pending activation. A rejected sequence-1 event was already consumed by the adapter and could not be recovered by a later successful activation retry.

The coordinator now owns a production-linked `EconomyActivationGate` whose readiness is returned to callers. Normal economy ticks are skipped while it is closed, the adapter checks it before draining pending Money or a dirty established Cash baseline, the combined profile/run publisher short-circuits while it is closed, and `HandleCurrencyFlow` enforces it again defensively. Initial Cash baselining remains allowed because it publishes no event. Buffered flows allocate no event identity until registration succeeds, so the first accepted flow remains sequence 1. If the bounded Money queue fills during a prolonged storage failure, Money capture is explicitly degraded instead of discarding an unpublished exact flow.

`ContinuedActivationSaveFailureRetainsSynchronousCashAndMoneyUntilSequenceZeroIsDurable` composes the real atomic repository, extracted production gate, and production-linked native adapter. It blocks the profile `.tmp` path, keeps that fault active through a Money callback, a synchronous raid Cash pickup, and repeated ticks, and proves that neither scope receives a flow and no currency row is created. After removing the fault, one tick durably registers sequence zero and publishes the retained Money and Cash exactly once as sequences 1 and 2; the repository cursor and totals agree.

### M9 partial-stack Cash and active-run Money-correlation correction

Further review found two attribution boundaries that the full-stack and base-context tests did not exercise. The capability disclosure now names this boundary. Reward and Money-sale semantic correlation applies only to events captured outside a run. During an active run, the exact Money flow retains its original conservative `Raid`/`UnknownAdjustment` attribution and valid run, segment, and map identities.

### M9 resumable profile-transition correction

Corrective review found that a queued profile transition retried its entire action after any later failure. Because `Open` and `Rotate` are durable, a capability-save failure after a successful generation change could replay the rotation, archive the just-created generation, and move intervening callbacks into unintended history.

Profile transitions now retain an ordered step cursor. Successful prerequisite flush, run interruption, identity refresh, `Open`, and `Rotate` steps are never replayed; retry resumes at the first failed step. `ProfileChanged` is published exactly once immediately after the repository switches generations and before later fallible capability persistence, so activation and baseline ownership move to the new generation even if post-transition persistence needs retry.

`FailureAfterDurableRotationRetriesTheRemainingStepWithoutRotatingAgain` uses the real repository and atomic store. It completes a save-deletion rotation, publishes the generation change once, blocks the new profile's temporary save path during `SetEconomyCapabilities`, verifies the transition remains pending at `generation-two`, removes the fault, and proves retry finishes in that same generation rather than consuming `generation-three` or republishing the change. Focused profile-transition coverage passes 2/2; the complete Debug and Release suites pass 665/665. `scripts/build.ps1` passes the installed Duckov contract, frame-time analyzer, warning-free Core/native Release builds, and package-directory validation. ## M10 lossless route-event association

### Frozen baseline and production-path audit

Fresh `origin/main`, local `main`, and the requested starting commit were all `fbb6b36a73b6f580d40d07c5829ac76ca47aa9fe`; `feat/lossless-route-association` was created directly from that commit. The only pre-existing working-tree artifact was the protected untracked `m8.1-performance-results.html`, exactly 53,825 bytes at SHA-256 `632bc80c1c62567be0d4221c854475566427cb2389d71cbadc3a743c176eb725`; it remains excluded from edits, source, and packaging. The baseline installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 compatibility probe, frame analyzer, warning-free native Release build, and package validation passed. The inherited 665-test suites had one documentation-only failure because the living README still asserted the obsolete M9 candidate status after v0.9.0 publication; production coverage was otherwise green, and M10 updates that living contract.

The 2,048 ceiling was the `SegmentEventAssociations` row limit in `RunLifecycleTracker`. When the next item-use, healing, firing, combat, or container row arrived, it retained the already reduced overall/segment statistic but disabled shared event-attribution and route-map capability, preventing later exact route publication. Equipment itself publishes stable segment loadout roots rather than association rows, but completed-run and route-map projections consume the same route-capability result. M9 economy is intentionally independent: it uses activation cursors and exact run/event-time-segment fan-out and neither needs nor writes shared rows. Combat and healing can keep different source/outcome segment IDs across a transition. The raw association list is not needed to recompute the accepted statistic totals or reject duplicates: per-family reducers and bounded live correlation/identity windows already own those contracts. Its consumers are current-schema validation, checkpoint/recovery evidence, capability presentation, JSON, `routes.csv`, `segments.csv`, and `segment_events.csv`.

Active dirty checkpoints are cloned on the main thread at the existing one-second/periodic cadence and serialized by the single-flight background writer. A measured empty schema-9 checkpoint was 4,583 bytes; the same shape with 2,048 raw association rows was 457,190 bytes, about 221 bytes per completed event and roughly 22 MB at 100,000 rows. An unbounded raw ledger was therefore rejected. A replay cursor alone was rejected because it cannot project exact event-family source/outcome segment counts. Per-event journal/watermark designs were rejected because they retain the same volume scaling and recovery/serialization cost. Exact aggregation is sufficient because no accepted M1-M9 projection requires the discarded per-event payload after the family and two stable segment identities are proven.

### Schema-10 design and deterministic evidence

Schema 10 retains the accepted `SegmentEventAssociations` JSON/CSV surface but gives each new row representation `ExactAggregate`, a checked 64-bit count, and first/last UTC timestamps. Rows are keyed by event family plus stable source and outcome segment IDs. Repeated visits to the same map remain separate; delayed combat and healing preserve distinct endpoints. A value-type dictionary key makes recording an indexed lookup without a concatenated string or historical scan. The maximum new cardinality is five families times the legitimate 64 by 64 segment pairs. Finite schema-9 rows migrate as `LegacyRaw` with count one. Unsaturated schema-9 profile and active-checkpoint candidates preserve exact evidence. A saturated candidate preserves surviving rows, marks `HistoricalEventAttributionIncomplete` with provenance, leaves whole-history exact attribution unavailable, and enables the separate `CurrentEventAttributionCapture` capability so later valid events continue exactly. Known current exact route-map values remain publishable alongside the historical marker rather than being replaced with invented zero or discarded.

Current-schema profile and checkpoint candidates validate association representation, count, IDs, segment joins, timestamp order, capability consistency, and historical-incomplete provenance before normalization. Malformed primary candidates lose atomically to intact backup candidates. Checked count overflow retains the previous exact value, marks historical incompleteness, and disables only current shared capture; it never wraps or reports a saturated number as exact. M9 economy retains its separate cursor and route capability and does not enter the schema-10 buckets.

Production-linked regressions cover exactly 2,048 and 2,049 accepted associations with identical supported semantics; 100,000 mixed firing, combat, item-use, and healing events across an A to B to A to C route; a separate post-2,050 late-family case including container and equipment evidence; correct late-family run, source/outcome segment, completed-run, starting-map, route-map, Runs UI, JSON, and affected-CSV projection; repeated-map identity; delayed endpoints; duplicate delivery; missing-join degradation followed by later exact capture; schema-9 unsaturated and saturated profile/checkpoint migration; schema-10 checkpoint reopen after association 2,049; invalid schema-10 primary fallback for profile and checkpoint; valid temporary aggregate-checkpoint selection over invalid primary and backup candidates; and stable serialized size from 10,000 to 100,000 events. The schema-10 checkpoint test deliberately blocks its atomic temporary path, proves the 2,049-item aggregate plus late firing/combat/container/healing rows fail to write, removes the fault, retries the same snapshot, recovers every family once, and proves a second reopen cannot duplicate the run or its totals. A composed tracker/scheduler test reduces 10,000 callbacks into one dirty snapshot opportunity and retains the one-second coalescing boundary for the next callback. Existing transition, terminal, clean-shutdown, combat correlation, container saturation, and economy-isolation regressions remain in the complete suite. Focused M10 coverage passes 15/15 at this checkpoint; performance, deployment, gameplay, restart, and delivery evidence follows chronologically after those gates run.

The pre-deployment automated candidate passes 15/15 focused M10 tests and the complete 679/679 Debug and 679/679 Release suites. The strengthened late-family test snapshots the still-active run after the 2,050-event primer, then proves firing, cross-segment combat, container, item, cross-segment healing, and equipment evidence in the active checkpoint, completed run, correct segments, complete starting-map total, route-map total, Runs UI predicate, JSON, association CSVs, and equipment CSV. `scripts/build.ps1` independently adds another green 679/679 Release run and passes the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract, warning-free FrameTimeAnalyzer and native Release builds, and exact five-file package-directory validation. The rebuilt five-file directory is byte-identical to the independently extracted candidate. Whole-solution formatting verification, `git diff --check`, and tracked forbidden-binary source safety pass. These are automated/package-preparation results only; matched-control performance, deployment, user-controlled gameplay, projection readback, restart, clean shutdown, push, CI, and draft PR remain open gates.

Implementation commit `2ff8451df6a9e484b2255e3f9aa3d0b3e799987e` produced the exact pre-deployment candidate. `UltimateDuckovStatistics-v0.10.0.zip` is 269,141 bytes at SHA-256 `52f9438b83a1b8eb61593592833048daafedbc8d486f107f5ad913ed8c64f690`; its canonical 103-byte sidecar is SHA-256 `de17e0422366e6b358c69c22c983a5a882b7552773cb9793a095805a03af98f0` and contains the same ZIP digest. Independent extraction contains exactly the permitted five files and matches the package directory byte-for-byte: `info.ini` 308 bytes / `4451a4f775946be9399220e5725a3757703be90259625b37f6cf49e60b4128a5`, `INSTALL.md` 14,590 / `48cdcfb131703772ccfb1b9623f3c560ba04e2e562ad90057cd24efffd3c869d`, `LICENSE` 1,117 / `0f7558f2469ad0901074f6c380ada1ed91861d55adf905267bc70b26cd2e3ccc`, Core DLL 442,368 / `e7cea3eab21305f38c3ef02795e3c2c35db0440b8fd9b629ea513829a696ec7b`, and native DLL 271,872 / `3314d497f2744f8562a1044da844e2d1ac17fdcae5deee425b9bb81a6c70da8d`. The three frozen Harmony-only `B/high-rate-single-enemy` controls remain hash-identical to their sidecars. ### 2026-08-19 pre-1.0 distribution and migration support decision

The product owner clarified that every UDS `0.x` build, tag, GitHub pre-release, and downloadable ZIP is a development artifact for voluntary testing. None is distributed through an officially supported installation channel. A user who manually downloads those artifacts may exercise their migration paths, but persisted-profile compatibility from one `0.x` version to another is best-effort development continuity rather than a supported end-user guarantee.

Existing schema migrations, fixtures, and exact-migration tests remain intentionally useful internal hardening. Earlier milestone wording that records an exact pre-1.0 migration result describes tested candidate behavior; it does not establish a supported upgrade contract. Supported upgrade and migration guarantees begin only with the first version explicitly declared as officially distributed through a supported channel, and that release will define its supported starting baseline.

Accordingly, the reviewer-reported issue whose only path was carrying UDS-owned persisted data from v0.9.0 into v0.10.0 was classified non-actionable under the product support boundary. This classification does not claim that the path works and must not be generalized to dismiss defects that also affect a clean/current installation, same-version current-schema operation or recovery, a future officially supported migration baseline, or data safety independently of the unsupported predecessor. No product-code correction or new compatibility claim follows from the discarded finding.

## M11 combat ownership and observed-death semantics

### Frozen baseline and corroborating production evidence

Fresh `origin/main` and the local starting point were `6edf6231a6a9596d3fbd82522b3aade566be88e0` (`docs: define v1 migration cleanup`, 2026-08-19); `feat/combat-ownership` was created directly from that commit. The only pre-existing working-tree artifact was the protected untracked `m8.1-performance-results.html`, exactly 53,825 bytes at SHA-256 `632bc80c1c62567be0d4221c854475566427cb2389d71cbadc3a743c176eb725`; it remains excluded from edits, source, and packaging.

The literal generation suffix in the requested export name (`1523690077194c7b3d2c960f20843eb`) was not present. The timestamp-matching retained export exists read-only under generation `1523690077194c07b3d2c960f20843eb` and contains the requested run `e4478f42a21446b5a210f94a8ed5cfae`. That run is an Extracted Nullpunkt to Lagerbereich route with zero completed player projectiles, zero ranged hits, zero player damage, and one old `enemies_killed` result. Its Wolf, PM weapon `duckov:weapon:784`, and `Environmental` ownership rows all retain the same fatal event, while `equipment_combat.csv` assigns that old kill to an otherwise empty loadout association. This is corroborating evidence of the M5-M10 ambiguity: weapon metadata and a fatal transition existed, but retained actor evidence did not prove a player final blow. The local export remains read-only and is not a test fixture.

### Installed native evidence and production design

The audit used the installed Escape From Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 assemblies and Harmony 2.4.1. `DamageInfo.fromCharacter` is actor evidence independent of `fromWeaponItemID`. `ItemAgent_Gun` initializes both projectile actors from the holder, then changes the credited actor to `CharacterMainControl.Main` only for the exact native `LevelManager.ControllingCharacter` owner chain. Projectile direct and explosion damage construct `DamageInfo` from that credited actor; reflection replaces both credited and physical actors with the reflector before subsequent damage. Melee performs the same controlling-character rewrite. `CharacterBuffManager.AddBuff` persists `Buff.fromWho` and `fromWeaponID`, and the damage/explosion effect paths copy those values into delayed `DamageInfo`. The native companion links available in this build are `LevelManager.PetCharacter`, `PetAI.master`, and `AICharacterController.leader`. `ZoneDamage.Damage` explicitly clears `DamageInfo.fromCharacter` immediately before `Health.Hurt`; it is the only audited path promoted to proven actorless `Environmental` evidence.

The M11 adapter therefore carries distinct credited, physical, and `DamageInfo` actors into one allocation-free policy. Consistent exact-main evidence is `Player`; the explicit bounded pet/master/leader chain is `Companion`; another consistent non-null actor is `Other NPC`; only the exact `ZoneDamage.Damage` scope with no actor is `Environmental`; missing or contradictory evidence is `Unknown`. A weapon or ammunition ID is never an actor input. Mutable projectile actors are refreshed at impact, while the initial proven-player state alone owns the completed-projectile accuracy denominator. Non-player and ambiguous fatal transitions become observed-world deaths, retain their target/cause/weapon context, and receive neither player damage nor player equipment-kill credit. A player headshot final blow additionally requires proven player ownership.

Schema 11 replaces the primary ambiguous total with `KillsByYou`, `ObservedWorldDeaths`, and explicit `LegacyUnclassifiedDeaths`. Migration traverses lifetime, completed-run, starting-map, route-map, and segment combat/equipment aggregates. Only retained Player ownership or the independently proven headshot-final subset becomes player kills; retained Companion ownership becomes observed-world deaths; the remainder and all old equipment kill credit become legacy-unclassified with historical provenance. Active schema-10 checkpoints migrate before save or recovery and before interrupted-run lifetime fan-out. Existing schema-10 compatibility members remain internal through M17 under the pre-1.0 policy; M18 owns their removal.

Deterministic M11 coverage exercises all five ownership results, controlling-character owner chains, actor conflicts, weapon-without-actor non-proof, player versus observed fatal classification, player-only headshot final blows, a penetrating projectile with separate headshot and non-headshot victims, delayed damage-over-time, equipment exclusion for every non-player/unproven owner, UI/profile/JSON/all affected CSV agreement, nested schema migration, active-checkpoint migration and interruption recovery, and allocation-free policy execution across 100,000 fatal transitions. Existing production-path regressions continue to cover duplicate delivery, delayed source/outcome segments, ordered repeated-map routes, shutdown/reopen, valid-backup and temporary recovery, generation rotation, M8.1 scheduling, and M10 bounded lossless association.

The automated checkpoint passes 10/10 focused M11 tests and the complete 688/688 Debug and 688/688 Release suites. `scripts/build.ps1` independently reruns the Release suite and passes the installed-game contract (including controlling-character, companion, buff-source, and `ZoneDamage.Damage` members), warning-free FrameTimeAnalyzer/Core/native Release builds, and exact five-file package-directory validation. Whole-solution formatting verification, `git diff --check`, and tracked forbidden-binary source safety also pass.

### Review correction: buff reapplication conflicts and penetrating headshot final blows

Review of the installed production path found two clean/current-install defects that supersede the earlier candidate artifact. First, native `CharacterBuffManager.AddBuff(Buff buffPrefab, CharacterMainControl fromWho, int overrideWeaponID)` refreshes the first existing same-ID buff's timer/layers without replacing its original `Buff.fromWho`. The old postfix reread only that retained actor and discarded the new callback actor. The corrected postfix forwards all three native arguments and a weak-keyed per-buff tracker retains the first observation, compares every incoming actor, and makes any missing or different reapplication monotonically conflicting. Effect and delayed-tick scopes carry that conflict into the ownership policy, which returns `Unknown` before considering the stale native actor. Player-first/NPC-second and NPC-first/player-second fatal ticks therefore record zero player damage, zero `KillsByYou`, one observed-world death, and no equipment combat association.

Second, one penetrating projectile scope retained a shot-level `HeadTargeted` flag. After target A consumed the one projectile headshot on a nonfatal transition, target B's later fatal transition could still satisfy the old final-blow predicate. The production classifier now derives ranged hit, headshot, and headshot final blow together for each transition. A headshot final blow requires the current fatal transition to consume the projectile's one proven headshot; a later victim cannot inherit it.

Focused correction coverage passes 38/38. It drives both same-ID actor orders, same-actor refresh and missing evidence, the shared projectile scope across A's nonfatal headshot and B's fatal transition, active-checkpoint recovery, lifetime aggregation, JSON, `combat_attribution.csv`, and empty `equipment_combat.csv` credit for the ambiguous buff outcome. The complete Debug and Release suites pass 692/692. `scripts/build.ps1` independently reruns Release and passes the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract, the exact three-argument buff callback, warning-free Core/native/FrameTimeAnalyzer builds, and exact five-file package-directory validation. Whole-solution formatting verification, `git diff --check`, and tracked forbidden-binary source safety pass. The correction changes only deterministic unsafe/unrealistic paths already assigned to native audit plus production-path regression evidence; it does not invalidate the completed user-controlled direct-player, Other-NPC Direct/DoT, UI, export, or clean-reopen evidence. Its ZIP and deployment are superseded by the buff-callback dependency correction recorded below.

### Review correction: combat dependency on the healing-owned buff callback

A further production-path review found that combat still consumed `CharacterBuffManager.AddBuff` observations only through `HealingHarmonyBridge`. Healing rejects and cleans up its complete three-method Harmony set when any one of `Health.AddHealth`, `EffectAction.NotifyTriggered`, or `CharacterBuffManager.AddBuff` has an unsafe foreign patch or later loses its exact patch stamp. Combat initializes independently. It could therefore keep its own health/effect hooks and report ownership, damage-over-time, player-kill, and observed-death metrics Supported after the only callback capable of detecting same-ID actor conflicts was absent. An unseen player-first/NPC-second refresh could then trust Duckov's stale Player `Buff.fromWho`; the reverse order could trust stale OtherNpc evidence.

The correction introduces one activation-scoped production trust object shared by the healing and combat adapters. Healing marks it trusted only after all three owned patches and stamps are installed successfully, and marks it untrusted before detaching any runtime hook for rejection, drift, failure, or disposal. Combat snapshots that state during initialization and checks the same allocation-free volatile flag before every buff/effect application scope and once per frame. Trust loss is monotonic for the combat activation because missed callbacks cannot be reconstructed. An untrusted parent buff resolves to missing/conflicting evidence, hence `Unknown`; current-equipment fallback and delayed association capture are suppressed. `DamageDealt`, `Ownership`, `WeaponIdentity`, `DamageOverTime`, `KillsByYou`, and `ObservedWorldDeaths` become `DisabledIncompatible`, while exact damage received, ranged hits/accuracy, melee metrics, player deaths, ammunition, and headshot metrics retain their independent support.

Focused combat/persistence coverage passes 43/43. It drives the linked production observation boundary through both initial rejection and later loss, with retained Player and OtherNpc actors in each state; attempts the unseen conflicting reapplication through that same capture boundary; exercises valid health/effect support with only buff observation unavailable; and proves `Unknown`, zero player damage, zero player kills, one observed death, no equipment association, disabled dependent capability states, checkpoint recovery, JSON, `combat_attribution.csv`, and empty `equipment_combat.csv` for both retained actor orders. The complete Debug and Release suites pass 698/698. `scripts/build.ps1` passes the installed native contract, warning-free builds, and exact package-directory validation; formatting, `git diff --check`, and tracked forbidden-binary source safety also pass.

### Review correction: combat dependency on the effect-trigger scope

A further patch-drift review found that `DamageDealt`, `Ownership`, `WeaponIdentity`, `KillsByYou`, and `ObservedWorldDeaths` remained Supported after combat lost its `ItemStatsSystem.Effect.Trigger` hook. The healing-owned `CharacterBuffManager.AddBuff` callback could have correctly retained a same-ID player/NPC actor conflict, but without the combat effect scope that conflict never reached `Health.Hurt`. Native `DamageInfo` then exposed Duckov's retained first actor and weapon ID, allowing a delayed fatal tick to be mislabeled Player or OtherNpc and to receive current-equipment credit.

The ownership completeness contract now requires exact `Health.Hurt`, trusted buff-application observation, and the combat-owned effect-trigger scope. Patch-stamp drift that disables `Effect.Trigger` immediately marks `DamageDealt`, `Ownership`, `WeaponIdentity`, `DamageOverTime`, `KillsByYou`, and `ObservedWorldDeaths` `DisabledIncompatible`; exact damage received, ranged hits/accuracy, melee metrics, player deaths, ammunition, and headshot metrics remain independently supported. Native buff/effect `Health.Hurt` transitions still carry `DamageInfo.isFromBuffOrEffect` even when the scope callback is absent. The health boundary uses that native flag to force the affected event to `Unknown`, clears the retained weapon identity, and allows no player or equipment credit. Direct projectile, melee, environmental, and damage-received observations are not relabeled merely because the aggregate ownership set is incomplete.

Focused combat/persistence coverage passes 47/47. It first drives both actor orders through the trusted production buff-observation boundary and proves the conflict was captured, then removes effect-scope trust before the health transition and supplies only the stale retained native actor and weapon. Both orders produce `Unknown`, an unknown weapon, zero player damage, zero player kills, one observed-world death, no equipment association, and disabled ownership-derived capabilities. The same state survives active-checkpoint recovery and JSON/CSV export. The complete Debug and Release suites pass 702/702; the installed Duckov 2.3.30 contract, warning-free Core/native/FrameTimeAnalyzer builds, exact package-directory validation, formatting, `git diff --check`, and tracked forbidden-binary source safety pass.

### Review correction: marker-independent missing effect scope and truthful projections

Installed-native review of Duckov 2.3.30 found that `ExplosionAction.OnTriggered` can recover its parent `Buff`, copy the retained `Buff.fromWho`, and set `DamageInfo.isExplosion` while leaving `DamageInfo.isFromBuffOrEffect` false. The previous missing-scope guard depended on that false marker, so losing the combat-owned `Effect.Trigger` patch could still allow a conflicting same-ID buff's retained Player actor and weapon through a fatal explosion. The health boundary now asks only whether another trusted ownership scope is actually present. If effect-scope observation is unavailable and no projectile, melee, environmental, or effect scope reached the transition, ownership becomes `Unknown` and native weapon fallback is suppressed regardless of Duckov's marker fields. The complete capability policy remains fail-closed until the activation restarts.

The native panel now uses one production-linked capability formatter for run headers, expanded segment rows, lifetime combat totals, enemy and ownership breakdowns, and weapon rows. Every disabled metric renders as `Unsupported`; legacy-unclassified history remains explicitly labelled. Append-only capability-state columns were added to `runs.csv`, `run_totals.csv`, `map_totals.csv`, `route_map_totals.csv`, `segments.csv`, and `equipment_combat.csv`. The rows retain partial raw totals for auditability but can no longer present them without their current availability state; JSON and `combat_attribution.csv` retain their existing capability contracts.

Focused correction coverage passes 8/8. Both retained actor orders drive a trusted same-ID conflict into marker-false Explosion classification after effect-scope loss, prove `Unknown`, unknown weapon identity, zero player damage/kills, one observed-world death, no equipment credit, and disabled ownership-derived capabilities, then survive route-bearing checkpoint recovery and export. Existing untrusted-buff cases now assert the same state in run, lifetime, starting-map, route-map, and segment CSV rows. Dedicated regressions prove disabled nonzero UI values format as `Unsupported` and a retained equipment-combat row carries every relevant disabled capability state. The complete Debug and Release suites pass 704/704. `scripts/build.ps1` and committed-head `scripts/create-release.ps1` pass the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract, warning-free Core/native/FrameTimeAnalyzer builds, and package validation. ## M12 world-time and sleep statistics

The installed-native audit is [docs/M12_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md](docs/M12_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md). Duckov 2.3.30 stores `long days` plus `double secondsOfDay`, advances days after `86,300.0` seconds, and fires `GameClock.OnGameClockStep` for ordinary progression, `StepTimeTil`, and load hydration. UDS therefore observes checked `Day * 86,300 seconds + TimeOfDay.Ticks` coordinates and treats the first observation in each save generation as baseline-only. `GameClock.Now` is intentionally excluded because it composes an ordinary 86,400-second `TimeSpan` day. `StepTimeTil` also uses an 86,400-second next-day request before the native 86,300-second wrap, so UDS counts its actual reported coordinate rather than assuming the nominal target was reached.

Installed `SleepView.Sleep(float minuts)` has no start or cancellation event. Its only clock mutation is the sole installed call to internal static `GameClock.Step(float)`, followed 0.5 real-time seconds later by public static `SleepView.OnAfterSleep`. The production adapter records a completed sleep only from an exact matching prefix/postfix advancement candidate consumed once by that later callback. Ordinary clock callbacks during the delay can add only to observed world time. Cancellation before confirmation, interruption after advancement but before completion, duplicate completion, overlapping candidates, mismatched advancement, teardown, and save-generation replacement cannot add sleep totals.

Deterministic coverage drives ordinary progress, native midnight, multi-day forward advancement, non-sleep fast-forward, load initialization, duplicates, generation replacement, backward/invalid coordinates, within-day and midnight-crossing sleep, cancellation/interruption, exact-once completion, mismatched advancement, reset, checked arithmetic, independent capability degradation, 100,000 coalesced observations, schema-11-to-12 migration, clean and interrupted persistence, slot/generation separation, primary/backup/temporary recovery, and UI/profile/JSON/CSV agreement. The contract probe also requires the installed day constant, public clock fields/event, internal exact sleep-step signature, `StepTimeTil`, and sleep-completion callback.

The pre-gameplay candidate checkpoint passes 31/31 focused M12 tests and the complete 734/734 Debug and 734/734 Release suites, whole-solution formatting, `git diff --check`, tracked forbidden-binary source safety, the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 contract, warning-free Core/native/FrameTimeAnalyzer builds, and exact five-file package validation. Production-linked coverage includes exact mutation retry plus the shared run/world-time cleanup gate: a failed final world-time publication retains the profile coordinator until both owners have actually cleaned up, so retry cannot lose its persistence dependency. It also covers a cadence split where the sleep clock jump is persisted before `OnAfterSleep`, multi-day sleep, and export/profile flush before serialization. Independent extraction under `artifacts/audit-m12-export-flush-20260820T1833069673823Z` matches the package byte-for-byte. ### M12 user-controlled gameplay protocol

Codex may build, deploy, and inspect evidence, but the user alone launches and controls Duckov or changes gameplay/save state.

1. With Duckov closed, the user identifies the intended progressed slot. Codex copies that slot's complete Duckov save family and current UDS profile tree to a separate timestamped read-only backup and verifies every copied file by size and SHA-256 before gameplay.
2. Cold-launch Duckov, confirm HarmonyLib and UDS are active, select the backed-up slot, then open UDS Overview and Diagnostics. Record the four initial M12 values and confirm all four native world-time capabilities are `Supported`.
3. Leave the sleep chooser without confirming once. Reopen Overview and verify completed sleep sessions and sleep-advanced time did not change.
4. Allow a small amount of ordinary world-clock progression. Reopen Overview and verify observed in-game elapsed time increased while sleep totals did not.
5. Complete one normal sleep for a clearly selected native duration. After control returns, reopen Overview. Completed sleep sessions must increase by exactly one; sleep-advanced time must increase by exactly the selected duration; observed in-game elapsed time must increase by at least that duration; calendar days advanced changes only when the native clock crosses a day boundary.
6. Export once, leave Duckov open for evidence readback, and report the selected slot, selected sleep duration, visible before/after values, capability states, and anything unexpected or perceptible.
7. After Codex confirms the live profile and 25-file JSON-plus-24-CSV export agree, close Duckov normally. Codex then verifies same-generation schema-12 persistence, clean-shutdown residue, Player.log diagnostics, and deployed-byte identity without editing Duckov saves.

Its 86,400-second request combined with the native 86,300-second wrap lands at 07:01:40 on the next native day. ### M12 review correction: monotonic publication and bounded durable writes

Post-delivery review found that the original one-second aggregate publication also marked the complete profile dirty. The shared writer consequently captured, serialized, forced, and atomically replaced the growing profile once per second during ordinary world-clock progression. The same publication deadline used `DateTime.UtcNow`, so a backward Windows-clock correction could retain ordinary progression and a completed sleep only in memory until wall time caught up or another explicit boundary flushed it.

The correction separates responsive publication from durability. Frame-rate observations still coalesce into one checked in-memory profile mutation per monotonic second, preserving the existing Overview behavior without dirtying the snapshot writer. A separate process-monotonic 30-second cadence marks the profile dirty during ordinary progression. Completed sleep bypasses that delay and requests durability immediately; export, native save collection, profile transitions, application quit, deactivation, and cleanup retain their existing synchronous publication and writer barriers. A failed publication or durability request remains pending and retries at a monotonic one-second cadence rather than once per frame.

Four new production-linked regressions compose the exact cadence with the world-time observation boundary and deferred snapshot writer. A one-hour, 60-observation-per-second run permits 119-120 profile writes; a conservative 512 KiB retained profile therefore produces no more than 60 MiB of logical serialized output per hour instead of 3,600 writes and 1.8 GiB. Separate cases prove a two-hour wall-clock rewind cannot stall one-second publication or the 30-second durability deadline, completed sleep/lifecycle paths can force immediate durability, and both periodic and forced failures retry after one monotonic second. The focused M12 set passes 35/35 and the complete Debug and Release suites pass 738/738. The native Release adapter build remains warning-free. Abrupt process or OS failure can lose progression observed during at most approximately 30 seconds of real process time: roughly 30 in-game minutes at Duckov's ordinary 60x world-clock rate, or more if a non-sleep fast-forward occurs inside that window. Successful sleep is not intentionally exposed to the periodic window. This deterministic persistence-shape gate directly covers the corrected write-amplification mechanism; it does not rewrite the historical M8.1 frame captures or replace the later v1 release-candidate live qualification.

### M12 review correction: new-game boot baseline handoff

Review of the installed `LevelManager.Start` order found a distinct clean-new-game lifecycle: native `GameClock.Load` publishes its hydrated coordinate, `OnNewGameReport` rotates the UDS generation, and `OnNewBoot` immediately advances the already-loaded clock toward 07:00. The prior generic `ProfileChanged` subscriber reset the tracker without a baseline, so the boot callback became baseline-only and the new generation lost both its elapsed delta and any crossed calendar day.

World-time profile transitions now have two explicit modes. Save-slot selection and save deletion clear the tracker and await the next native load observation, avoiding attribution of a prior slot's clock. UDS-only reset and `OnNewGameReport` clear the tracker and immediately establish the new generation's baseline from the already-loaded current coordinate; `OnNewBoot` then remains a normal forward observation. The production-linked sequence regression executes `Load -> OnNewGameReport -> OnNewBoot` through the resumable profile-transition boundary and proves a 400-second native crossing contributes exactly one calendar day and 400 observed seconds to the new generation, with no sleep totals. Focused M12 coverage passes 36/36, and the complete Debug and Release suites pass 739/739.

### M12 review corrections: slot-load gate and retry-safe boot handoff

The selected-slot finding is reachable because installed `SavesSystem.SetFile` raises `OnSetFile` without reloading the existing `GameClock`; that prior instance continues publishing frame callbacks until scene construction creates and loads its replacement. Resetting the tracker at profile-commit time therefore allowed a stale callback to become the selected generation's baseline. Slot and deletion handlers now begin a transition-ID-scoped handoff synchronously inside the native event. The handoff retains the prior generation's already-queued aggregate for its required transition flush, rejects every callback whose `GameClock.Instance` is still the captured prior object, and accepts hydration only after the public static instance changes. A replacement load that arrives before a delayed UDS profile commit is staged with its later forward deltas and transferred only when that matching transition commits.

The retry finding is independently reachable because the resumable transition can return after a failed persistence or rotation step, while installed `LevelManager.Start` immediately continues from `OnNewGameReport` to `OnNewBoot`. The adapter now snapshots the already-loaded coordinate in the synchronous report-start notification, diverts subsequent clock callbacks into an isolated checked aggregate, and transfers the exact boot delta only after the matching new generation is ready. A completion from an older queued transition cannot consume a newer handoff. The original correction temporarily rejected sleep candidates throughout an active profile handoff; the later completed-sleep handoff correction below replaces that conservative behavior with exact transition-tagged staging.

Three new production-boundary regressions cover `OnSetFile -> completed UDS transition -> stale prior-instance callback -> replacement-instance Load`, replacement Load plus forward movement before a deferred selected-profile commit, and failed new-game transition step -> native boot advancement -> successful retry. A fourth proves a superseded completion ID cannot consume the newer handoff. The new-game case retains exactly one crossed native day and 400 observed seconds in the new generation with zero sleep totals. Focused world-time coverage passes 39/39; the complete Debug and Release suites pass 742/742. The installed-game contract probe now also requires the public static `GameClock.Instance`; Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam 24013657 / Unity 2022.3.62f2 / Harmony 2.4.1 passes, as do warning-free native/analyzer builds and exact package validation.

### M12 review correction: completed sleep survives a deferred profile handoff

### M12 review correction: multiple FIFO-queued profile handoffs retain separate totals

The installed slot selector can invoke another `SavesSystem.SetFile` while an older UDS profile transition remains queued after a real persistence or checkpoint failure. The prior singleton world-time handoff reset the older transition as soon as the newer native selection event arrived, permanently discarding its staged clock and completed-sleep totals. Handoffs now mirror the coordinator's FIFO lifetime: the second native boundary takes a final checked observation of the outgoing target clock, freezes the older transition into a fixed-size transition-ID snapshot, and starts a new active replacement-clock gate. Later callbacks route only to the newest handoff, while each queued completion can retrieve only its own frozen or active state and transfer it to the generation that has just committed.

The production-boundary regression executes slot A -> slot B Load -> one-hour completed sleep -> failed B transition -> slot C selection -> stale B callback rejection -> C Load -> two-hour completed sleep -> successful FIFO retries. The first retry commits exactly 3,600 observed seconds, one sleep, and 3,600 sleep seconds to B without consuming C. The next retry commits exactly 7,200 observed seconds, one sleep, and 7,200 sleep seconds to C. Focused world-time coverage remains 42/42 because this stronger case replaces the prior superseded-ID-only test; the complete Debug and Release suites remain 745/745.

### M12 review correction: same-slot selection retains the active clock

Installed `SaveSlotSelectionButton` invokes `SavesSystem.SetFile(index)` even when the selected index is already active. That method recaches the save and raises `OnSetFile` without loading or replacing `GameClock.Instance`. Treating every callback from the captured instance as stale therefore left a same-slot/same-generation reopen waiting indefinitely for a replacement clock that could never arrive, silently omitting later ordinary advancement and completed sleeps while all four capabilities remained `Supported`.

The handoff now buffers captured-instance and replacement-instance observations in separate checked aggregates while the profile open is unresolved. The completed repository open may reuse the captured clock only when the event-time and opened slot and generation IDs match and the open neither created nor rotated the profile. That proven same-profile path transfers the captured interval and restores its latest reading as the live baseline. A changed slot or generation retains the replacement-instance load gate. The event-time comparison also prevents an older FIFO-queued transition from making a later selection look same-profile merely because its repository step committed first.

The production-linked regression executes same-slot `OnSetFile`, a non-created/non-rotated reopen of the same generation, ten seconds of advancement during the handoff, then another 50 ordinary seconds and an exact one-hour completed sleep on the unchanged clock. It proves exactly 3,660 observed seconds, one completed sleep, and 3,600 sleep-advanced seconds in the profile, export document, `world_time.csv`, and UI while also rejecting different-slot and different-generation reuse. Focused world-time coverage passes 43/43, and the complete Debug and Release suites pass 746/746.

### M12 review correction: queued reuse uses immediate pre-open state

The prior same-slot correction classified a queued `OnSetFile` transition against the repository slot and generation captured when the native event arrived. A deferred A-to-B transition can remain ahead of a second B selection in the FIFO while Duckov has already loaded B's clock. When the first transition later opens B, the second transition's enqueue-time A snapshot is stale. It selected the changed-profile completion, treated the already-loaded B instance as its prior clock, and then waited indefinitely for another replacement. The same stale closure could also attempt to refresh A's identity after the repository had become B, keeping the second transition queued on a different-slot identity error.

Selected-slot open is now one retryable production operation. Immediately before each queued open runs, it reads the repository's then-current slot, refreshes only that current profile when the target differs, snapshots the then-current generation, opens the event-time target identity, and classifies reuse from those pre-open values plus the open result. This supersedes the enqueue-time comparison described in the preceding correction. Actual A-to-B hydration still completes through B's replacement-clock aggregate; the second B open sees B immediately before execution and completes through the captured-current-clock path.

The production-linked regression executes `A -> deferred B open -> B GameClock.Load -> second B OnSetFile -> FIFO commits -> unchanged B clock advances and sleeps`. It proves the two queued opens classify `[changed, same-profile]`, only A's identity is refreshed, both transitions leave the handoff inactive in B's generation, and the unchanged B clock records ten seconds during the queue, 50 later ordinary seconds, and one exact one-hour completed sleep. Profile, export document, `world_time.csv`, and UI agree on exactly 3,660 observed seconds, one completed sleep, and 3,600 sleep-advanced seconds with supported capabilities. Focused world-time coverage passes 44/44; the complete Debug and Release suites pass 747/747.
