### M12 review correction: pre-open identity survives a partial open retry

The execution-time reuse correction still captured its pre-open slot and generation inside the same retryable action that called `ProfileRepository.Open`. `Open` is not transactional: it can close A, load or create B, assign B to `Current`, and then fail while atomically writing B's `session.json`. Retrying the whole action recomputed its supposed pre-open identity from the already-mutated B repository, allowing an A-to-B transition to masquerade as a same-profile B reopen before B's replacement clock had loaded.

The coordinator now uses two separately resumable transition steps. The first refreshes the then-current profile and stores an immutable slot/generation snapshot. Only after that step succeeds and the transition cursor advances does the next step enter `Open` and classify its result against the frozen snapshot. Any number of partial `Open` retries therefore retain the original execution-time pre-open identity without falling back to enqueue-time state.

The production-linked fault regression starts in A, freezes A/generation-A, and creates a directory at B's `session.json.tmp` from the session-ID callback after `Open` has already assigned B/generation-B to `Current`. The first retry fails at the real atomic session write. After the directory lock is removed, the same open step retries before any B clock replacement and returns a non-created, non-rotated B result, but reuse remains false against frozen A. The handoff stays active, rejects the still-current A clock, treats B day 20 / 1,000 seconds as baseline-only hydration, and records exactly the next five seconds with zero false calendar days and Supported elapsed capability. Focused world-time coverage passes 45/45; the complete Debug and Release suites pass 748/748.

### M12 review correction: nested backup recovery retains the replacement-clock gate

Installed corrupt-save recovery can call `RestoreIndexedBackup`, raise an inner `SavesSystem.OnSetFile`, return to the outer `SetFile`, and raise another `OnSetFile` before Duckov loads the selected slot's replacement `GameClock.Instance`. The inner transition opens B, so repository slot/generation identity alone makes the outer transition appear to be a same-profile B reopen even though its captured clock still belongs to A. Restoring that unhydrated instance would baseline B from A and then either count B's load as false cross-slot advancement or disable B's clock metrics as backward movement.

Every awaiting-load handoff now records a dependency when it supersedes an unresolved handoff that has not observed a replacement instance. FIFO completion resolves that dependency as hydrated only when the older transition proves same-profile reuse or already staged a replacement clock. A changed older transition propagates its outstanding replacement requirement through any newer queued handoffs. A nominal same-profile repository result therefore cannot restore the captured clock while that gate remains; it marks the target profile ready, continues rejecting the prior instance, and lets only the selected slot's later replacement load establish the baseline. A companion regression proves nested callbacks that both resolve to an already-hydrated same profile still release the dependency and retain all checked intervals.

The production-linked backup-recovery regression executes `SetFile(B) -> inner OnSetFile -> outer OnSetFile -> stale A callback -> FIFO opens -> stale A callback -> B GameClock.Load`. The repository decisions are `[changed, same-profile]`, but the clock handoff remains active after both opens, rejects A, treats B day 20 / 1,000 seconds as baseline-only, and records exactly the next five seconds with zero calendar days and Supported elapsed capability. ### M12 review correction: ordinary scene replacement clocks rebaseline safely

The prior generation-only tracker therefore treated a normal replacement load as backward movement and permanently disabled calendar-day and observed-elapsed capture.

The world-time handoff boundary now tracks the last live clock instance even when no profile handoff is active. The first callback from every different instance takes and preserves the existing pending aggregate, clears the old tracker baseline and incomplete sleep candidate, establishes the replacement coordinate as baseline-only, then restores the exact pending totals. Profile-change completion records its proven replacement or retained current instance as live so the ordinary-instance rule composes with the selected-slot gates. Every adapter path performs the typed Unity destroyed-object null comparison before converting `GameClock` to `object` identity; a destroyed Unity object therefore cannot survive as a false non-null clock through managed-reference semantics.

The production-linked regression executes old base clock baseline -> 60 seconds -> final-save coordinate -> 20 seconds of outgoing post-save advancement -> old instance destruction -> replacement same-generation clock load at the saved coordinate -> five forward seconds. The replacement load returns `BaselineEstablished`, contributes no mutation, never enters the backward path, preserves the previously observed 80 seconds, and records the next five seconds for an exact pending total of 85 seconds with zero calendar days. ### M12 review correction: shutdown drains active profile handoffs

A retrying `OnSetFile` or `OnNewGameReport` transition can own target-generation clock advancement and completed-sleep proof that has not yet transferred into the ordinary observation boundary. Quit previously flushed only that ordinary boundary and the current repository; later cleanup reset the still-active handoff. If the original writer/open fault had already cleared but no `Update` retry ran before quit or deactivation, an otherwise recoverable current-schema mutation was discarded.

The resumable profile-transition boundary now has a terminal drain that repeatedly advances queued transitions until the queue is empty or a retry makes no progress. Application quit and deactivation invoke that drain before world-time publication and repository flush. World-time cleanup also owns a coordinator drain barrier: if a transition remains blocked, cleanup returns false before detaching or resetting any handoff with uncommitted aggregate or completed-sleep data. The existing retryable cleanup owner retains both adapter and coordinator; because world-time completion subscriptions remain attached when the initial drain fails, a later same-process cleanup retry can commit the transition, transfer its mutation, flush it, and only then dispose the coordinator. Baseline-only or incomplete unproven sleep state does not masquerade as a committed statistic.

The production-linked shutdown regression stages one hour of target-clock advancement and one exact completed one-hour sleep, then fails the queued profile transition. After the fault clears it invokes the terminal cleanup drain directly without an intervening `Update`, transfers and flushes the mutation, invokes cleanup a second time to prove idempotence, closes cleanly, and reopens the selected profile. The reopened current-schema profile contains exactly 3,600 observed seconds, one completed sleep, and 3,600 sleep-advanced seconds. ### M12 review correction: changed-profile shutdown discards stale prior-clock data

A selected-slot handoff can buffer ordinary callbacks from A while its repository transition is retrying. If the fault clears during shutdown, the terminal drain can commit B before B's replacement `GameClock.Instance` loads. That changed-profile completion proves A's buffered interval is permanently ineligible for B, but the prior cleanup guard still classified the buffer as uncommitted. World-time cleanup therefore retained the coordinator, prevented `ProfileRepository.CloseClean`, and left B's `session.json` to be recovered as a false interruption on the next launch.

Changed-profile completion now discards the captured prior-clock observation buffer as soon as the target profile is ready without a staged replacement clock. It retains only the prior instance identity needed to reject stale callbacks until B hydrates; callbacks from that instance after commit remain non-mutating. The cleanup guard therefore continues protecting target-generation clock and sleep mutations while allowing an intentionally ineligible prior-slot interval to be discarded and B's session checkpoint to close normally.

The production-linked regression executes `A baseline -> select B -> transition failure -> A advances -> fault clears -> shutdown drain commits B without replacement load -> later A callback -> cleanup -> reopen B`. It proves the handoff remains active solely as a replacement-instance gate, owns no uncommitted data, removes B's primary/backup/temporary session artifacts, reports no interrupted-session recovery, keeps `InterruptedSessionCount` at zero, and records none of A's movement in B. ### M12 review correction: retained cleanup keeps a stable generation provider

The world-time adapter's mutation, durability, capability, and cleanup-barrier delegates were bound directly to the newly created profile coordinator, but its generation provider closed over the mutable `ModBehaviour.profileCoordinator` field. Setup now captures the newly created coordinator through a production-linked stable generation-provider binding and uses the same local instance for all world-time wiring. Releasing the behavior field therefore cannot invalidate the retained adapter. The cleanup completion gate still owns disposal order: the old coordinator remains open until world-time and run cleanup finish, then closes exactly once before a replacement owner activates.

The production-wiring regression executes `A baseline -> select B -> B replacement load -> one-hour advancement and completed sleep -> transition write failure -> deactivation cleanup failure -> behavior field release -> fault clearance -> same-process TryCleanupPending`. The retained coordinator drains the transition, the completion subscriber reads B's generation without failure, the exact `3,600 / 1 / 3,600` mutation persists, the session closes without interruption recovery, and a replacement process-lifetime owner assigns and cleans up successfully. ## M13 crafted-item statistics

The installed-native audit is [docs/M13_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md](docs/M13_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md). On Duckov 2.3.30, public `CraftingManager.Craft(string)` resolves a formula and awaits private `Craft(CraftingFormula)`. The private task returns `null` for insufficient cost or failed payment, otherwise awaits `Cost.Return` output delivery before invoking downstream callbacks and returning its generated-item buffer. UDS correlates the exact `Cost.Return(false, true, 1, generatedBuffer)` task to that private invocation and records one action at successful delivery completion, before downstream subscribers run. The singular declared `result.amount` is quantity; numeric result ID, formula ID, and declared batch size are retained independently. The per-chunk `OnItemCrafted` callback, inventory movement, ingredients, price, and currency are intentionally excluded.

The installed build exposes no persisted craft queue, cancellation callback, delayed collection state, reliable workstation ID, run/map context, or multiple-output formula. Incomplete tasks at process exit record nothing. Null results and exceptions before delivery abandon their in-flight token; a downstream callback exception cannot erase a proven delivery. Overlapping tasks may finish out of order without sharing evidence; a token can publish only once; and a fresh process cannot consume a stale token. Workstation, context, and multiple-output capabilities remain explicitly `DisabledIncompatible`.

### M13 pre-gameplay candidate checkpoint

The candidate is based on authoritative `origin/main` commit `df46dbd5f345c976bbe01c84b776a60a708d4186`. Focused crafting coverage passes 38/38. Coverage includes request-versus-completion separation, null/failure/incomplete abandonment, out-of-order overlap, duplicate rejection, completion-time generation assignment, missing-generation rejection without stranded state, unknown/modded identity and fallback preservation, restart, exact action/quantity/recipe/batch aggregation, weighted batch-integrity recovery, missing supported recipe-composition and invalid capability-state fallback, independent arithmetic degradation, structured-key collision resistance, more than 2,048 distinct persisted and pending outputs, atomic pending-overflow rejection, capability degradation with truthful historical subsets, preservation of event-time proof across retry-time capability degradation and mixed proof scopes, schema-12 migration, current-schema recovery, restart and slot/generation isolation, JSON/CSV/UI agreement, the three-owner crafting/world-time/run cleanup gate, and the proof-to-publication outstanding state that prevents concurrent cleanup from disposing the profile dependency in between.

### M13 user-controlled gameplay protocol

Only the user controls Duckov and its saves. With the audited candidate deployed, qualification is:

1. Cold-launch Duckov, confirm HarmonyLib and UDS `0.13.0` are active, select the intended save, and open the F8 panel outside a raid.
2. In Diagnostics, confirm crafting completion actions, produced quantity, output identity, recipe identity, and batch metadata are `Supported`; confirm workstation identity, run/map context, and multiple-output recipes are `DisabledIncompatible`.
3. Record the Overview and Crafting baselines. Opening and closing crafting without completing a request must leave both totals unchanged. An unaffordable or otherwise naturally failed request, if safely available, must also leave both totals unchanged.
4. Complete one declared-quantity-one recipe. Actions must increase by exactly one and quantity by exactly one under the correct output and recipe.
5. Complete one recipe whose declared output amount is greater than one, if naturally available. Actions must increase by exactly one, quantity by the declared amount, and the batch distribution by one action at that amount. Repeat one available recipe and confirm exactly one additional action per completion.
6. Export once. `statistics.json`, `crafting_totals.csv`, and `crafting_recipes.csv` must agree with Overview and Crafting for totals, output identity, recipe identity, batch distribution, capability state/provenance, and pre-M13 historical unavailability.
7. Close Duckov normally and report the crafted output names, recipe IDs if visible, declared amounts, any skipped optional case, UI behavior, and any perceived hitch. Post-gameplay inspection then verifies profile/export/log agreement, clean shutdown, deployed bytes, and absence of transactional residue without launching or controlling the game.

### M13 review correction: terminal shutdown abandons unproven crafting tasks

The initial cleanup path correctly retained the crafting adapter and profile coordinator while any wrapped native task was outstanding. That is required for ordinary mod deactivation, where the task can still finish in the same process. Application quit used the same retry-only rule, however, even though an incomplete `Cost.Return` task cannot be resumed after process exit. The three-owner cleanup gate could therefore retain the coordinator through termination, leave `session.json`, and classify the next otherwise-normal launch as an interrupted UDS session.

Application quit now invokes a terminal-only crafting cleanup path. It first stops accepting requests and latches terminal state, then abandons only task tokens that have not crossed the correlated post-delivery proof boundary. Already-proven mutations remain in the retryable accumulator and must flush before patch cleanup succeeds. A late task continuation observes the terminal latch and returns its native result without reading a disposed profile. Ordinary deactivation still abandons nothing: its process-lifetime owner remains pending until the native task finishes or fails, and completion directly retries that owner so the shared crafting/world-time/run gate can close the retained coordinator.

### M13 review correction: independent delivery and retryable capability publication

The installed private craft path completes every output delivery inside `Cost.Return(false, true, 1, generatedBuffer)` and only then invokes the public `OnItemCrafted` multicast. A built-in telemetry or third-party subscriber can therefore throw after the player has received the complete declared batch. The original wrapper observed only the final private task result and abandoned that request on any exception, silently undercounting delivered output while current capture remained `Supported`.

The native adapter now opens one request scope in the private-craft prefix, wraps only the exact `Cost.Return` task invoked inside that scope, and claims the action at successful delivery completion before downstream callbacks. Private-task null/failure still rejects unproven work; a later subscriber exception is passed back unchanged without retracting the already-published statistic. Both patch surfaces have exact owner/stamp validation and runtime drift checks. Deterministic regressions cover delivery proof before a simulated subscriber exception plus missing and duplicate return correlation.

A separate review path reproduced transient capability persistence failure. Crafting capability publication now stages a cloned, revisioned payload before every attempt and clears it only after the publisher returns successfully. Periodic processing retries it, while export, profile transition, shutdown, and cleanup barriers force an immediate attempt and refuse completion while the payload remains pending. Capability and aggregate publication are attempted independently, so either recovered sink can advance without being suppressed by the other. Regressions inject `IOException`, prove the payload survives, prove barrier retry publishes it, and prove an older successful write cannot clear a newer degradation snapshot.

### M13 review correction: save-transition handoff and durable status links

Duckov changes its selected save before raising `SavesSystem.OnSetFile`, while UDS may retain the predecessor generation if a queued boundary or repository step must retry. A craft delivered during that interval must not read the still-open predecessor generation. Crafting now begins a transition-ID handoff synchronously at the native save-selection event. Proven completions are aggregated under the newest active handoff, remain unavailable to ordinary profile publication until the corresponding FIFO transition opens its target, and are then rewritten to that committed generation before retryable publication. Prior aggregates and already-completed handoffs still flush at transition barriers, while active handoffs do not deadlock the target open. Export, deactivation, and terminal cleanup refuse to discard uncommitted handoff data; terminal draining may still complete and publish an already-staged handoff after the terminal latch.

Focused regressions cover a delivery while the profile transition boundary is deliberately stalled, publication only after target-generation commit, persisted verification that the predecessor profile remains unchanged, retained publication retry, and overlapping selections routing later delivery to the newest transition. Durable README, release-note, and plan prose no longer embeds a live draft/latest-release snapshot; it links to GitHub pull-request, tag, and release surfaces for mutable state.

### M13 review correction: user-reset crafting handoff

`ResetCurrent()` rotates the current schema-13 profile into a read-only archive and creates a fresh generation through the same retryable transition queue used by native save changes. The initial profile-handoff correction covered slot selection, save deletion, and new-game rotation but omitted this user-reset entry point. A proven delivery during a retained reset could therefore publish against the still-open predecessor immediately before that generation was archived, leaving the successful craft absent from the new current profile and every UI/export projection.

User reset now allocates a profile transition ID and publishes the crafting handoff start before queueing any flush/writer/rotation step. After `UserReset` rotation opens the fresh generation, a paired completion event transfers staged output to that exact generation before the general `ProfileChanged` publication. The stalled-reset regression blocks the transition boundary, completes a Tierfalle while the predecessor remains open, resumes rotation, and proves the fresh current generation contains exactly one action/quantity one while the read-only archived predecessor remains at zero.

### M13 review correction: production-linked user-reset regression

The initial stalled-reset test called `CraftingProfileHandoffBoundary.Begin` directly and manually assembled `NativeProfileTransitionBoundary` steps. The regression no longer begins the handoff or enqueues its own transition. Removing either production handoff boundary, changing its transition ID, or moving completion before rotation makes the existing fresh-generation/archive assertions fail.

### M13 review correction: atomic capability snapshots

Runtime crafting degradation previously updated the generic adapter records and the lifetime crafting metric-capability projection through separate profile saves. A successful generic write followed by a failed metric write could therefore leave a current-schema profile whose Diagnostics view showed capture disabled while the lifetime projection still claimed `Supported`; a restart could then present the skipped interval as exact.

`NativeProfileCoordinator` now publishes the complete generic, economy, world-time, and crafting capability snapshot through one `ProfileRepository.SetCapabilitySnapshot` call and one atomic profile save. The former generic-only repository API is gone, and slot selection, save deletion, new game, and user reset use the same snapshot boundary. The regression first publishes supported crafting capabilities, degrades them, proves the entire publication advances the profile by exactly one revision, then simulates an interrupted same-schema reopen and verifies all eight generic crafting records and the lifetime completion capability remain `DisabledIncompatible` with the exact degradation provenance.

### M14 user-controlled gameplay protocol

Only the user controls Duckov and its saves. After explicit approval and transactional deployment of this audited candidate:

1. Cold-launch Duckov, select the intended save, open the F8 panel outside a raid, and confirm pairing, character-slot state, and nested equipped-item slot state are `Supported` in Diagnostics. Record the Combat and Equipment baselines.
2. In one run, use one weapon with two safely available ammunition types and perform a known number of accepted firing actions with each. Do not interpret the result as ammunition consumed or projectiles created.
3. On one equipped weapon, retain an occupied attachment interval, remove the attachment while the native slot remains present for a clear active-raid interval, then install an attachment again. Record the root slot, weapon, native nested slot, and child names shown.
4. On one safely available equipped non-weapon item, perform the same occupied-to-proven-empty-to-occupied sequence in a nested attachment-style slot. Also retain representative occupied and proven-empty character-slot intervals. Skip any unavailable or unsafe case and report it explicitly; do not manufacture an item or alter a save for the test.
5. Avoid counting pause and loading as active duration. After returning safely, compare the weapon-first pair counts/percentages, per-character-slot weapon time, exact configuration rows, named-child rows, and explicit `No <slot>` rows in the UI, then export once.
6. Close Duckov normally. Restart the same save, confirm the same persisted values in the UI, export again if convenient, and close normally a second time.
7. Report the weapon/ammunition IDs or names, attachment and non-weapon paths used, approximate active intervals, skipped cases, UI behavior, and any perceived hitch. Post-gameplay inspection will then compare the schema-14 primary/backup profile, both exports' `statistics.json`, `weapon_ammunition_pairs.csv`, `character_equipment_slots.csv`, `equipped_item_nested_slots.csv`, `Player.log`, deployed five-file hashes, and cleanup residue without launching or controlling the game.

### M14 review correction: partial nested-slot evidence isolation

The nested-slot builder deliberately treats a null native slot entry as incomplete evidence while retaining readable roots and siblings. The legacy M6 attachment-signature traversal subsequently re-enumerated the same mutable native slot collection with an unconditional `value.Key` dereference. That exception escaped the builder into the adapter-wide observation boundary, which suspended the complete equipment snapshot before the M14 nested capability could degrade independently.

`AddAttachments` now orders by the nullable slot key and skips null entries. Its exact-set signature remains unchanged for every fully readable collection, while partial nested evidence reaches the existing per-item and aggregate completeness gates. The production-linked regression wires `NativeEquipmentAdapter` to a real active `RunLifecycleTracker`, supplies one fully readable equipped root plus a second equipped root whose nested collection contains both a null entry and a readable sibling, and advances five active seconds. On the original traversal it records two whole-observation invalidations and fails. With the correction it records zero invalidations; both character roots and the unaffected root's nested child each accumulate exactly five seconds, character-slot capability stays `Supported`, nested-slot capability becomes `DisabledIncompatible`, and no nested state is fabricated for the incomplete parent.

### M14 review correction: completeness-only snapshot publication

The adapter deduplicated an observation by route context plus `SnapshotId`, but the builder's slot-state contribution omitted aggregate and per-parent completeness flags. Growing one equipped tree from exactly 256 retained empty slots to a 257th slot preserved the first 256 rows, loadout identity, attachment signature, and prior snapshot identity while changing nested evidence from complete to incomplete. Adapter-level capabilities degraded, but `snapshotHandler` was suppressed; the active run and route segment therefore retained `Supported`, and no checkpoint mutation was requested.

Snapshot identity now canonically includes character-slot completeness, aggregate nested-slot completeness, and each equipped parent's nested completeness alongside the retained root and nested states. Loadout identity remains unchanged. A completeness-only change therefore reaches `EquipmentStatisticsReducer.Observe`, applies the existing independent capability restriction to both run and segment aggregates, replaces the current snapshot, and marks the active checkpoint dirty without fabricating the unretained slot.

The production regression wires `NativeEquipmentAdapter` to a real active `RunLifecycleTracker` and `ProfileRepository`. It first publishes an equipped backpack with exactly 256 empty native slots and verifies run/segment nested capability is `Supported`. It appends empty `Slot256`, raises the observed character item's production item-tree notification, and verifies only the first 256 rows remain retained, loadout identity is unchanged, snapshot identity changes, checkpoint mutation advances, character-slot state stays `Supported`, and nested-slot state becomes `DisabledIncompatible` in both the run and segment. The active-run checkpoint is then saved and reopened through interrupted-run recovery; both recovered capability projections remain unavailable. The regression fails on the original head because the checkpoint's current snapshot stays complete and passes with the correction.

### M14 review correction: namespaced equipment-slot UI preservation

The native snapshot builder correctly classifies only exact built-in root keys as weapon slots, so a modded key such as `mod:PrimaryWeapon` is retained as `duckov:slot:mod:PrimaryWeapon` with item kind Other. The equipment UI projection subsequently discarded that evidence because its built-in weapon-slot filter compared only the final colon-delimited suffix. The slot therefore appeared in neither Weapons nor Armor & Gear.

Built-in weapon-slot recognition now removes only the trusted `duckov:slot:` or legacy `slot:` prefix and rejects a remaining namespace separator before comparing the native weapon keys. Exact built-in and legacy weapon slots retain their prior filtering, while namespaced unknown/modded slots remain available to Armor & Gear. The production-path regression sends an occupied `mod:PrimaryWeapon` slot and a genuine empty `PrimaryWeapon` slot through `NativeEquipmentAdapter`, a real `RunLifecycleTracker`, run reduction, and `EquipmentStatisticsViewModelFactory`. The original implementation fails with an empty Armor & Gear collection. The correction retains the modded row as Other with its exact five-second duration, continues to filter the true built-in empty slot, and creates no false weapon row.

### M14 review correction: display-only metadata publication

Equipment snapshot identity deliberately excludes mutable localized and mod-provided display text, but the production adapter also used that identity as its complete observation deduplication key. Changing an installed root, slot, or nested item's display metadata without changing TypeID, membership, state, or completeness therefore built an enriched snapshot that never reached the active run. The prior fallback could remain in checkpoints, UI, JSON, and CSV until an unrelated equipment transition occurred.

The native adapter now maintains a separate canonical display-metadata signature alongside the stable snapshot identity. A display-only change publishes the new snapshot while preserving `SnapshotId`. On the reducer's same-identity path, only already-observed rows are enriched: no duration row or slot evidence can be created, active durations do not advance at the same timestamp, and `TransitionCount` remains unchanged. The current run and route segment both return a mutation to the lifecycle tracker, so the active checkpoint becomes dirty immediately.

The production regression wires `NativeEquipmentAdapter` through a real `RunLifecycleTracker`, active checkpoint, completed `RunReducer`, `EquipmentStatisticsViewModelFactory`, and `StatisticsExporter`. It first observes a held modded weapon and nested optic with blank native display metadata, advances five seconds, then mutates the public root-slot, item, nested-slot, and child display fields and raises the production item-tree notification without changing identity or time. The original adapter fails because the checkpoint mutation revision does not advance. The correction proves the checkpoint advances while snapshot identity and transition count stay fixed, root and child durations remain exactly five seconds, and every enriched name reaches the Weapons view, serialized JSON, `character_equipment_slots.csv`, and `equipped_item_nested_slots.csv`.

### M14 review correction: current-schema orphan-pair recovery

Current-schema recovery validated pair structure and reconciled counts against each marginal row that existed, but it did not require the pair's weapon and ammunition identities to exist in their corresponding marginal dictionaries. Removing either marginal made that reconciliation loop vacuously succeed. A valid JSON primary could therefore retain an exact weapon-ammunition pair with no matching weapon or ammunition aggregate and defeat an intact backup.

`WeaponStatisticsReducer.ValidateAggregate` now requires every pair's weapon ID and ammunition ID to be present in their respective dictionaries before count reconciliation. This is a non-repairable current-schema invariant: production pairing records a pair only after both supported identities create their marginals, while unsupported or incomplete identity evidence remains in the existing explicitly uncorrelated projections and never creates a pair.

The regression links and invokes the production `NativeWeaponFireAdapter`, raises its native public firing event for one exact weapon/ammunition action, records it through a real `RunLifecycleTracker`, completes it through `ProfileRepository`, and persists a valid primary and backup. It then removes exactly one weapon or ammunition marginal from the serialized primary, saves that checksum-valid candidate atomically, and reopens through `ProfileRepository.Open`. On the original validator both cases fail because `RecoveredSnapshot` is false. With the correction the primary receives a semantic M14-association failure, the intact backup is selected and repairs the primary, and the recovered profile retains the exact pair plus both marginal totals.

### M14 review correction: canonical weapon marginal identities

The initial orphan-pair correction required each pair ID to exist as a weapon and ammunition dictionary key. The marginal validators still accepted a nonblank row whose internal `WeaponId` or `AmmunitionId` differed from that key. Pair reconciliation grouped by the row values, while pair lookup and membership used the keys; a primary could therefore retain an apparent pair membership that had no corresponding marginal identity in UI or `weapon_totals.csv` and still defeat its intact backup.

Current-schema validation now requires every weapon dictionary key to equal its row's `WeaponId` and every ammunition key to equal its row's `AmmunitionId` using ordinal comparison. Pair membership therefore joins canonical identities rather than merely occupied dictionary positions. This check precedes pair reconciliation and atomic candidate selection, and it rejects the inconsistent current candidate without mutating or normalizing it.

The existing production regression now covers four corruptions after generating one exact action through `NativeWeaponFireAdapter`, `RunLifecycleTracker`, and `ProfileRepository`: missing weapon marginal, missing ammunition marginal, weapon row replaced under the original key with a different nonblank identity and valid zero totals, and the equivalent ammunition replacement. On the prior frozen head the two mismatch cases fail because `RecoveredSnapshot` remains false. With the correction all four primaries receive semantic M14-association failures, the intact backup wins, and the recovered pair plus both original marginal totals remain present.

### M14 review correction: readable nested siblings under partial evidence

The native builder retains each readable nested path when another entry on the same equipped parent is null, blank, duplicated, over-depth, or over-limit, and marks that parent plus the aggregate nested family incomplete. The reducer nevertheless filtered duration preflight, accumulation, and display enrichment to parents whose full tree was complete. One damaged sibling therefore discarded every occupied and proven-empty path that the snapshot had individually proved on that parent.

Completeness now gates the nested-family capability rather than readable path truth. The reducer preflights and accumulates only the `NestedSlots` actually present on every retained parent and enriches only matching existing rows. It does not reconstruct an omitted entry, infer a missing key, or turn unreadable evidence into an empty state. `NestedSlotState` remains `DisabledIncompatible` with its incomplete-evidence provenance while each retained occupied or proven-empty sibling accrues its exact parent-observable duration.

The production regression sends an equipped backpack with one null native nested entry followed by a readable occupied `Cube` sibling through `NativeEquipmentAdapter`, a real `RunLifecycleTracker`, `ProfileRepository` completion and clean reopen, and `StatisticsExporter`. On the original reducer the checkpoint contains only the complete armor parent's child and fails to find the backpack sibling. The correction retains the `4:Cube/` / `duckov:item:901` occupied row for exactly five seconds in the checkpoint and reopened lifetime aggregate. Every exported backpack row carries the same path, item, duration, and `DisabledIncompatible` capability; JSON retains `Readable sibling`; and neither persistence nor CSV contains an empty backpack row for the null entry. Direct reducer coverage also proves readable occupied and proven-empty paths survive incomplete parents and remain overflow-checked.

### M14 review correction: recoverable current slot-state capability

An incomplete character or nested slot enumeration correctly restricted the active run, but `NativeEquipmentAdapter` treated that observation-specific limitation as a process-lifetime incompatibility. Later complete snapshots could publish retained state yet never restore the adapter's current CharacterSlotState or NestedSlotState. Because `NativeRunLifecycleAdapter` samples the adapter capability at each `ControlReady`, every subsequent clean raid in the same activation began unavailable until the mod restarted.

Slot-state availability now follows the latest successfully built snapshot. An incomplete collection publishes the existing unavailable provenance, while a later complete collection restores the verified supported provenance for current capture. This promotion is intentionally limited to the adapter snapshot presented to future runs: `RunLifecycleTracker` continues to restrict an already-affected run and segment, so later readable evidence cannot rewrite the historical incomplete interval.

The regression links and production-composes `NativeEquipmentAdapter` and `NativeRunLifecycleAdapter` with `ProfileRepository` across two extracted raids in one activation. Raid one starts with a valid backpack/Cube path plus one null character root and one null nested entry, then receives a complete tree at five seconds before extraction. Raid two starts clean one second later and extracts after another five seconds. On the original adapter the second run fails because both slot-state capabilities remain `DisabledIncompatible`. With the correction, the first run remains degraded, the second run and current profile capability records are `Supported`, and the root plus nested path each retain exactly five seconds in both run aggregates and their CSV rows with the correct per-run capability state.

### M14 review correction follow-up: run-start capability refresh ordering

The first capability-recovery correction made a complete active-run observation restore the adapter's current slot-state availability. Its regression repaired the tree before raid one extracted, so it did not cover the production ordering when raid one ends incomplete. Outside a run, `ObserveNow` cleared its cache and returned without reading completeness; at the next raid, `ModBehaviour.Update` ticked `NativeRunLifecycleAdapter` first, which copied the stale disabled value into `RunStartContext` before the equipment watchdog could observe the repaired tree. The later complete snapshot could restore only the live adapter record because run and segment aggregates correctly remain restrict-only.

Inactive item-tree notifications and watchdog observations now build a capability-only snapshot: they update current CharacterSlotState and NestedSlotState but never publish a loadout, duration, empty state, or invalidation into an inactive run. In addition, the production `equipmentCapabilitiesProvider` calls `CaptureCapabilitiesForRunStart`, which synchronizes the current main duck and refreshes slot completeness synchronously before `NativeRunLifecycleAdapter` constructs `RunStartContext`. This closes the lifecycle-first frame even when no base notification or equipment watchdog ran after the repair.

The rewritten production regression extracts raid one at five seconds while both a null character root and null nested entry are still present. It then removes those invalid entries while inactive without raising an equipment notification and proves the live capability remains stale-disabled immediately before raid two. The production lifecycle tick invokes the new provider boundary, restores both families before `ControlReady`, and starts raid two as `Supported`; the following ordinary equipment publication records the clean tree for exactly five seconds. Through `ProfileRepository` and `StatisticsExporter`, raid one remains disabled, raid two and current profile capability records remain supported, and character plus nested CSV rows retain their exact five-second durations and per-run capability states. Separate coverage proves a normal inactive item-tree notification also restores both live capability records while producing zero run snapshots and zero invalidations.

### M14 review correction: ambiguous duplicate slot keys

Character-root and nested-slot enumeration previously detected a duplicate key only after emitting the first occurrence. Conflicting native or modded slots could therefore create an exact-duration row for whichever occurrence sorted first, even though the authoritative root or path identity was ambiguous. The same behavior became externally visible for nested slots once readable siblings from incomplete parents were correctly retained.

Snapshot construction now materializes each readable collection and preflights all nonblank keys before emitting any state. Every occurrence of a duplicated character-root or nested key, including the duplicated subtree, is omitted and its capability family is degraded. Null and blank entries retain their existing independent incomplete-evidence behavior. Unique roots and nested siblings continue to publish; no omitted duplicate is inferred as empty. The native adapter contract version advances to `public-item-tree-v10`.

The production regression drives conflicting duplicate `ModRoot` character slots and conflicting occupied/empty `Cube` children, together with unique Armor, Backpack, and occupied `Pouch` siblings, through `NativeEquipmentAdapter`, a real `RunLifecycleTracker`, `ProfileRepository`, clean reopen, and `StatisticsExporter`. On the original builder, the first `ModRoot` state persisted for five seconds. With the correction, both slot-state capability families are `DisabledIncompatible`, the unique roots and `Pouch` path retain exactly five seconds, no duplicate-key root or nested row reaches the checkpoint, persisted aggregate, JSON/CSV projections, or empty-state output, and no whole-observation invalidation occurs.

### M14 review correction: incomplete-root exact-set isolation

Null, blank, and duplicate character roots correctly made the M14 character-slot collection incomplete while retaining individually readable siblings. Snapshot construction nevertheless hashed that retained subset into ordinary M6 loadout and active-totem-set IDs. Those IDs accrued exact durations, entered bounded transition history and recurring rankings, and were captured by shot/combat callbacks even though an omitted weapon, direct totem, or other root could change the authoritative whole set.

The native builder now emits `duckov:equipment:unavailable` for both exact-set IDs whenever character-root enumeration is incomplete, while retaining readable root and nested state for M14. The live adapter independently degrades EquipmentSlots and DirectTotems in addition to CharacterSlotState, restores them after a later complete enumeration for future scopes, and advances its contract to `public-item-tree-v11`. The reducer uses the explicit unavailable sentinel as the exact-identity boundary: it accrues no loadout or totem-set duration, does not fabricate recurring rows, and suppresses partial-to-partial exact transitions. Complete M6 snapshots remain keyed by their explicit exact IDs, including older synthetic/current-schema fixtures whose later M14 completeness member is defaulted.

The production regression composes `NativeRunLifecycleAdapter`, `NativeEquipmentAdapter`, `NativeWeaponFireAdapter`, `ProfileRepository`, clean reopen, and `StatisticsExporter` across two native-started and extracted raids. Each raid has a readable held PrimaryWeapon with an occupied Scope path plus conflicting duplicate `ModRoot` entries, one of which is a direct totem, and fires once through the real public callback. On the original code the first failure is EquipmentSlots remaining `Supported`; the same subset would also generate a two-occurrence recurring loadout. With the correction, the readable root and Scope retain exactly five seconds per raid and ten seconds lifetime, both exact-set capability families are `DisabledIncompatible`, all loadout/totem-set duration and recurring projections remain empty, partial exact transition count remains zero, and every firing association retains the proven selected weapon while publishing unavailable loadout and totem-set IDs through persistence, JSON, and CSV.

Immutable correction commit `9142d88dc041e8fb4caa900448cfed590a394f74` passes the focused production regression, the M14 category at 35/35, and the complete Debug and Release suites at 839/839. Both DLLs report file version `0.14.0.0` and product version `0.14.0+9142d88dc041e8fb4caa900448cfed590a394f74`.

## M15 current economy holdings implementation and user qualification

The feature branch starts from `origin/main` commit `00ff242d565f706b9c4345464ddb0712e343d53a`. Before implementation, the installed Duckov 2.3.30 / Steam build 24013657 assemblies were decompiled and inspected for Money ownership and hydration, Cash ownership roots and loading, inventory/storage/pet change surfaces, save collection and selection, level/scene replacement, ATM conversion, reset, restart, and shutdown. The verified binary hashes and exact semantic boundary are recorded in [docs/M15_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md](docs/M15_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md). The executable probe now gates the private `Int64` Money field and save payload, load/generate/setup surface, `EconomyData` key check, exact three-root Cash helper, root/loading/change contracts, and ATM Save/Draw methods in addition to the prior contracts.

Schema 15 stores independent Money and Cash observations with exact generation ownership, UTC observation/freshness evidence, and `Current`, `LastObserved`, or `Unavailable` state. Current-schema candidate selection rejects missing roots, mismatched generation evidence, invalid state/value/timestamp combinations, and a current value without a supported current capability. Valid current values downgrade to last-observed after restart. Schema-14 migration explicitly marks holdings unavailable and does not inspect or reconstruct them from M9 flows. Primary, backup, temporary, interrupted-session, clean restart, rotation/reset, and prior-generation rejection tests use the production repository and atomic serializer.

The production adapter observes the hydrated `EconomyManager` and the exact top-level main, PlayerStorage, and PetProxy inventories. Its one-tick gate coalesces duplicate callbacks; incomplete hydration performs only constant-time readiness checks; Cash enumeration is identity-safe, checked, and bounded by the installed storage loader's 16,384-entry capacity; and an unchanged total produces no profile revision or persistence request. Production-composed tests cover non-zero activation without flow, proven zero, all three hydration gates, stack/internal-movement stability, Money and Cash changes, same-instance selection without `EconomyData`, generation replacement, scene staleness/reconfirmation, independent capabilities, version incompatibility, overflow, cleanup, UI/JSON/CSV agreement, and blank unavailable exports. The save-switch regression links and initializes the actual `NativeProfileCoordinator` plus `NativeEconomyHoldingsAdapter`, registers a native Money loader first, exercises synchronous `SavesSystem.SetFile`, and reads the production atomic primary/backup files.

Focused M15 qualification passes 36/36. After the user-controlled matrix exposed the independent M9 PetProxy teardown defect, focused M9 passes 108/108 and the complete Debug and Release suites each pass 877/877. Whole-solution `dotnet format --verify-no-changes` and `git diff --check` pass. The installed-game compatibility probe, warning-free FrameTimeAnalyzer/native builds, tracked forbidden-binary scan, schema/product-version assertions, exact five-file package verification, immutable-head hashes, independent extraction, deployment readback, and CI evidence were re-run for the correction and recorded on the pull request and implementation task.

User-controlled qualification passed on 2026-08-29 under [docs/M15_MANUAL_VALIDATION.md](docs/M15_MANUAL_VALIDATION.md). Slot 1 generation `d78609c23dd341ac9ca4265c7f19e337` cold-hydrated at Money `98,959`, Cash `0`, and liquid wealth `98,959`; Duckov's ATM matched. A one-unit withdrawal produced Money `98,958`, Cash `1`, and unchanged liquid wealth, while main-to-storage-to-pet movement changed neither holdings nor flow. Main-menu state retained timestamped last observations and unavailable liquid wealth. A clean restart began last-observed, then restored Money `98,958`, PetProxy Cash `1`, and liquid wealth `98,959` to current without a startup flow. The 31-file schema-15 export, live profile, JSON, and holdings CSV agreed; both shutdowns closed cleanly with byte-identical primary/backup, no UDS temporary residue, and no UDS error-like `Player.log` line. No gameplay, save selection, ATM action, inventory transfer, save write, or shutdown was controlled by Codex.

That matrix proved a separate M9 production path: `SceneLoader.onStartedLoadingScene` precedes old-root destruction, while the earlier M9 adapter suspended only at the next `LevelManager.OnLevelBeginInitializing`. Cash retained in PetProxy was therefore observed as a false Base outflow during base-to-menu teardown. `SceneLoadingStartSuspendsCashBeforePetInventoryTeardown` invokes the linked production adapter and fails on `public-events-v11` with one `Cash Outflow 1`; `public-events-v12` passes by flushing and suspending at scene-load start. `SceneLoadingStartFlushesLegitimateCashBeforeSuspension` proves a real dirty delta is still published before that suspension. The M15 holdings remained correct throughout and its completed live matrix need not be repeated for this bounded M9 correction.

The post-qualification production-composition review also reproduced native-first save switching: slot A Money `111`, native synchronous load of slot B Money `222`, then UDS transition processing. Before the correction, `NativeMoneyLoadBeforeUdsSaveSwitchCannotCrossContaminatePriorGeneration` failed because slot A's primary persisted `222`. `authoritative-roots-v3` now receives a transition-ID-bearing holdings-only start event before any transition flusher, while the general `ProfileChanging` event keeps its prior queued timing. The regression passes with slot A primary and backup retaining `111`, slot B remaining unavailable before its matching `ProfileChanged`, and slot B becoming and persisting current `222` afterward. `OverlappingSaveSlotHandoffsRemainSuspendedUntilEveryMatchingCompletion` proves deferred or nested handoffs cannot reopen observation between transition IDs.

A same-schema recovery review then found that the M15 insertion had attached the existing checked-crafting `OverflowException` boundary to holdings validation. `CurrentSchemaCraftingCompositionOverflowLosesPrimarySelectionToIntactBackup` writes a valid schema-15 backup, replaces the primary with two individually valid crafted-output rows whose checked composition overflows, and calls the production `AtomicJsonStore<ProfileDocument>.Load` candidate selector. The frozen faulty head throws before backup inspection; the correction classifies the primary as invalid crafting state and loads the intact backup. This deterministic recovery regression requires no gameplay or Duckov save manipulation.

The reset production-composition regression starts a clean native save with no `EconomyData` key, lets native `OnMoneyChanged` make Money `333` authoritative, and invokes the actual `NativeProfileCoordinator.ResetCurrent()` path with the linked holdings adapter and repository. The prior implementation fails because the unchanged manager plus missing key leaves the reset generation `Unavailable`. `authoritative-roots-v3` records reset-specific trust by transition ID and exact manager instance; the corrected reset generation and both atomic profile copies retain Money `333` as `Current`. The native-first slot-switch, overlapping-handoff, and untrusted same-instance regressions continue to pass, proving that reset trust cannot weaken save-slot isolation. This deterministic UDS-owned reset correction requires no gameplay or Duckov save manipulation.

## v0.16.0 crafting-resource consumption qualification

M16 was implemented from `origin/main` commit `e3a05147188a80d65f75dfbe2d3c554ee48187c9` on `feat/crafting-resource-consumption`. The installed Duckov 2.3.30 audit establishes that private `CraftingManager.Craft(CraftingFormula)` checks `Enough`, calls `formula.cost.Pay()`, awaits the output `Cost.Return(false, true, 1, generatedItems)`, and only then reaches M13's proven successful-delivery callback. Native `Cost.IsFree`, `EconomyManager.IsEnough`, and `ItemUtilities.ConsumeItems` all treat `Cost.items == null` as an exact empty item cost. `EconomyManager.Pay(Cost)` checks affordability, charges currency, queues one item-removal closure per non-null declared `Cost.items` entry, executes those closures, returns detached children through `SendToPlayer`, and invokes its paid callback. Repeated IDs are checked independently, and every closure captures the same pre-removal durability-ordered item references. Because `DestroyTree` marks an item synchronously but defers Unity destruction, a later closure can count the same captured stack again; pre-removal stock therefore cannot prove actual combined removal. A returned same-ID child can also decrease during `Item.Combine` without being consumed because the destination stack increases first by the same quantity. M16 observes Duckov's matched `GetItemCount`, `Item.StackCount`, and `Item.MarkDestroyed` operations and requires both sufficient affordability evidence and checked net ownership-ending mutation quantity exactly equal to the repeated declaration; same-ID transfers net to zero. It adds no inventory query and never reconstructs a quantity from holdings. The event-time cost snapshot is captured from the exact invoked formula before payment and committed only when M13 proves delivery and any required repeated-entry proof succeeds; it is never reconstructed from inventory deltas, a new holdings scan, historical action counts, or later recipe metadata. The full native audit, hashes, serialized-formula inventory, and currency limitation are recorded in [docs/M16_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md](docs/M16_NATIVE_CONTRACTS.md).

Schema 16 persists identity-preserving resource totals and recipe/output associations, plus independent total currency charges. Item-resource identity, output association, total currency, and Money/Cash split have independent capability records, history availability, arithmetic exactness, and provenance. Repeated item entries are coalesced with checked arithmetic at the boundary only after their combined quantity is proven. A successful craft with unproven or insufficient repeated-entry resource evidence marks only resource history incomplete; M13 completion totals and independently proven currency remain truthful. Schema-15 migration preserves existing crafting totals while marking pre-M16 item-resource and currency history unavailable, without reconstructing either. The installed contract proves the declared total currency charge but not its Money-versus-physical-Cash split, so the split remains explicitly unavailable.

The focused M16 acceptance matrix is deterministic and invokes production composition where required:

- `EventTimeCostsFlowThroughProductionBoundaryRepositoryPersistenceAndExports` proves event-time snapshot isolation, repeated-resource canonicalization, exact modded identity, repository mutation, atomic persistence/reopen, JSON, and CSV.
- `InsufficientCombinedStockDuplicateCostDoesNotPublishCanonicalDeclaredQuantity` invokes the production Harmony craft, payment, affordability, stack-mutation, destruction, delivery, and task callbacks through `NativeCraftingAdapter` into the actual `NativeProfileCoordinator`; the faithful native payment stub starts with one four-unit stack for `X x 3; X x 3`, removes only four, retains the successful output and 150 currency, but publishes no six-unit resource claim in the profile, JSON, or CSV and marks only resource history unavailable.
- `SufficientSingleStackDuplicateCostPublishesOneCanonicalSixUnitConsumption` drives the same production composition with one six-unit stack. The first installed-order closure reduces it to three, the second destroys the remaining three, and the matched distinct mutations prove one exact six-unit resource action, supported resource capability, and matching CSV projection.
- `DistinctFullStacksRepeatedCostDoesNotPublishDoubleCountedDestruction` uses two distinct, consistently durability-ordered three-unit items. Both native closures capture both references; the first marks the first stack, the second sees that deferred-destruction object again, and only three distinct units are removed while the other stack remains. The production adapter retains output and currency but emits no exact six-unit resource row in profile, JSON, or CSV. All three controls execute exactly the installed two entry scans; the UDS proof adds no scan.
- `DetachedSameIdChildTransferDoesNotCompleteRepeatedCostProof` extends the same faithful deferred-closure composition with a detachable same-ID six-unit child on the first three-unit cost stack. After both duplicate removals, the stub returns that child through the installed destination-increase-then-incoming-decrease merge order. The surviving stack increases from three to six, the child decreases from six to three and remains owned, and only the first cost stack's three units end ownership. The production adapter therefore preserves output and currency, marks resource history incomplete, and emits no false six-unit profile, JSON, or CSV row.
- `DefaultFreeCostRetainsResourceCapabilityAndLaterItemCostRemainsExact` drives a successful default-cost formula through the production Harmony callbacks, adapter, coordinator, and persistence boundary; proves null items emit no resource mutation and retain both resource capabilities; then proves a later two-unit item cost remains exact in the same generation.
- `AbandonedOrUnprovenCraftNeverPublishesItsCapturedCosts` proves abandoned and unproven attempts publish no costs.
- `FreeItemOnlyMoneyOnlyAndCombinedCostsRemainIndependentWithSharedResourceAssociations` covers free, item-only, money-only, combined, shared-resource, and multiple-recipe/same-output cases.
- `OverlappingBatchedCraftsAndDuplicateCompletionKeepEventTimeCostsIsolated` covers multiple resources, non-unit batches, out-of-order overlap, duplicate completion, and exact per-token cost isolation.
- `ProfileHandoffRetainsCostPayloadAndRebindsOnlyItsGeneration` and `SlotNewGameAndResetKeepM16CostsInTheirExactSaveGeneration` prove the complete cost payload crosses the production handoff intact and remains isolated across slot selection, new game, and user reset.
- `SchemaFifteenCraftingTotalsRemainWhilePreM16CostHistoryIsUnavailableAndNotReconstructed` and `SerializedSchemaFifteenMissingM16CapabilityMembersMigratesWithoutClaimingRepairOrHistory` cover migration truthfulness and legitimate missing schema-16 members.
- `ResourceAndCurrencyArithmeticDegradeIndependentlyWithoutLosingOtherExactMetrics` and `CompletionActionOverflowAlsoStopsDependentResourceAndCurrencyActionCounts` cover checked arithmetic and dependency boundaries.
- `UnprovenSuccessfulCostEvidenceMarksOnlyItsIndependentHistoryIncomplete` and `UnprovenCurrencyEvidenceMarksOnlyCurrencyHistoryIncomplete` cover independent resource/currency capability degradation and partial-history semantics in both directions.
- `OneHundredThousandCraftsCoalesceToBoundedAggregateStateWithoutRawHistory` covers the bounded aggregate/performance constraint.
- `CurrentSchemaResourceCompositionMismatchLosesPrimarySelectionToIntactBackup` exercises the real atomic current-schema candidate selector and backup recovery.
- `CurrentSchemaValidationRejectsExactZeroPairsAndAcceptsArithmeticUnavailablePairs` rejects exact zero/nonzero resource and currency pairs plus both inverse currency arithmetic-unavailable shapes, while retaining the reducer-produced action-frozen, amount-frozen, and both-dimensions-unavailable states.
- `CorruptExactResourceActionPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` drives a successful craft through the production Harmony callbacks, native adapter, coordinator, repository, and atomic writer; corrupts only the primary association action; and reopens through a new production coordinator to prove the intact backup wins and repairs the primary.
- `DegradedResourceFanOutSubsetPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` creates exact resource and currency data through the same production composition, degrades both resource capabilities, corrupts only the primary recipe/resource quantity, and proves a new coordinator rejects the impossible subset, selects the intact backup, and repairs the primary.
- `DegradedCurrencyFanOutSubsetPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` independently degrades currency capability, corrupts only the primary recipe currency amount, and proves exact lifetime/output/recipe equality is restored from the intact backup without disturbing exact resource evidence.
- `InverseCurrencyActionArithmeticPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` and `InverseCurrencyAmountArithmeticPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` persist real 150-currency production crafts, corrupt all three primary fan-out levels into the impossible inverse shape for one arithmetic flag, and prove a fresh coordinator selects the intact backup and repairs the primary.
- `SaturatedZeroLifetimeResourcePrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` persists a real six-unit-resource, 150-currency craft, zeroes only the primary lifetime/association quantities and marks resource quantity arithmetic unavailable, then proves a fresh coordinator restores generation, quantity, action, currency, and primary contents from the intact backup.
- `ExactResourceActionsExceedingQuantityPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` persists two exact six-unit, 150-currency crafts, corrupts only the primary lifetime/association quantities from twelve to one, and proves a fresh coordinator rejects two actions across one exact unit, restores the intact backup, and repairs the primary while the saturation-direction control remains valid.

Focused M16 tests pass 27/27 and the complete Debug and Release suites each pass 904/904. Whole-solution formatting and `git diff --check` pass. The installed-game compatibility probe reports 269 formulas, 681 item-cost entries, no empty item arrays, no repeated item TypeID within one installed formula, a maximum of six item entries, and 14 formulas with nonzero currency cost. User-controlled deployment, gameplay, restart, UI, and live-export qualification passed under [docs/M16_MANUAL_VALIDATION.md](docs/M16_MANUAL_VALIDATION.md); deterministic production composition covers recovery, repeated-entry affordability, net native stack mutation/destruction evidence, same-ID child transfer, native default/free costs, degraded fan-out corruption, directional arithmetic corruption, zero lifetime-resource corruption, impossible exact resource pairs, and other unsafe-to-induce cases. No Duckov process, gameplay action, save selection, save write, or shutdown was controlled by Codex, and no independent review is claimed.

### M16 review correction: impossible exact resource/currency zero pairs

The review froze branch head `0999f3a07b31678cf693af3be1f38ed3f1763dea` and reproduced two current-schema validation gaps. The companion reducer test proved that fully composed exact resource `actions > 0, quantity = 0` and currency zero/nonzero pairs could also pass even though `ValidateMutation` rejects them at the production boundary.

Current-schema validation requires every exact resource association dimension to be positive, every exact lifetime resource quantity to be positive, and exact currency actions and amount to be either both zero or both positive at lifetime, output, and recipe levels. Arithmetic-unavailable currency pairs are directional: reducer-produced `actions = 0, amount > 0` after action overflow and `actions > 0, amount = 0` after amount overflow remain valid, while their inverse shapes are invalid unless both currency dimensions are unavailable. ### M16 review correction: repeated deferred-destruction stack identity

The review froze branch head `0448cbc1cc3a0eae85dac855eda60f2636711f4e` and demonstrated that combined pre-removal stock was still insufficient proof for repeated item-cost entries. A full-stack closure detaches the item and calls `DestroyTree`; installed `DestroyTree` calls `Item.MarkDestroyed()` synchronously but schedules Unity destruction for later. With two ordered three-unit stacks and `X x 3; X x 3`, both closures capture both objects, the first marks the first stack, and the second sees that same non-null object with `StackCount == 3` and marks it again. Native payment succeeds while the second real stack remains, so only three distinct units were removed despite six units of initial stock.

The repeated-entry proof now requires two independent native surfaces inside the exact matched `EconomyManager.Pay(Cost)` call: the existing per-entry affordability observations and the checked quantity of distinct native stack mutations. A positive `Item.StackCount` reduction contributes only its before/after difference. The first `Item.MarkDestroyed()` contributes the current positive stack quantity; a later mark of the same synchronously flagged object contributes nothing. Exact resource evidence requires the resulting mutation total to equal the canonical checked declaration. No inventory query, before/after holdings scan, or persisted mutation journal was added. Patch trust and periodic drift inspection now include the public `StackCount` setter and `MarkDestroyed()` contracts; missing, conflicting, exceptional, under-mutated, or over-mutated evidence fails only the item-resource dimensions closed while retaining successful output and independently proven currency.

The production-composition regression uses a faithful native payment stub rather than injected scalar counts. ### M16 review correction: same-ID returned-child transfers

The review froze branch head `70b1a2de085d6703612af08b6941bc5cace6672f` and identified that the repeated-entry proof credited every positive `StackCount` reduction but ignored increases. Direct decompilation of installed `ItemUtilities.ConsumeItems(Cost)` confirms that each removal closure detaches children from a consumed slot-bearing item and queues them until all deferred removals finish, then returns each child through `SendToPlayer`. Installed `Item.Combine(Item incoming)` first increases the destination `StackCount` and then decreases the incoming `StackCount` by the same transferred quantity. A same-ID decrease during that transfer is not consumption.

With two ordered `X x 3` cost stacks, repeated `X x 3; X x 3` entries, and a detachable same-ID `X x 6` child on the first stack, the duplicate closures can mark only the first cost stack while leaving the second cost stack alive. Returning the child partially merges three into that survivor: the destination becomes six and the child becomes three, so all six child units remain owned. The frozen proof ignored the destination increase, credited the child decrease plus the first destruction, and could publish a false exact six. `duplicate-pay-proof-v3` instead accumulates every valid same-ID stack mutation as checked signed `before - after`, so both merge sides net to zero, and adds each stack's first positive ownership-ending destruction. A zero-count destruction caused by a full merge contributes zero. Exactness still requires the final net to equal the checked canonical declaration; mismatch fails only item-resource evidence closed while retaining successful output and independently proven currency.

`DetachedSameIdChildTransferDoesNotCompleteRepeatedCostProof` enters through the production craft, payment, affordability, stack setter, destruction, delivery, adapter, and coordinator composition. Its faithful payment stub constructs both durability-ordered closures before running either, detaches the same-ID child, repeats the deferred-destruction reference, then executes the installed destination-increase-before-incoming-decrease merge ordering. It proves owned quantities six and three remain, the checked net proves only three removed, resource history becomes incomplete, and no resource row reaches profile, JSON, or either resource CSV while output and 150 currency remain. The single six-unit exact control, two-stack deferred-destruction control, and insufficient-four-unit control remain green. Focused M16 Debug and Release pass 21/21; complete Debug and Release pass 898/898. The installed base formula collection contains no repeated resource ID, so this deterministic modded-identity regression requires no gameplay or save manipulation and supersedes the prior package/deployment.

### M16 review correction: degraded current-schema fan-out equality

The review froze branch head `48a1c26686012244e92ff0a74eff108efe2eb955` and identified that current-schema validation relaxed atomic M16 fan-out equality to subset checks when the corresponding resource or currency capability was degraded. The production reducer cannot emit such subsets: one proven resource event updates the lifetime resource quantity and recipe association together, and one proven currency event updates lifetime, output, and recipe together. Unproven evidence updates none of those levels, while checked overflow preflight skips the complete affected dimension. Capability degradation can make retained history partial, but it does not undo one level of an already committed fan-out.

Current-schema validation now always requires resource lifetime/association quantity equality and currency lifetime/output/recipe action and amount equality. Intentional arithmetic-unavailable zero/nonzero pairs remain valid only in the direction created when the affected dimension was skipped; the inverse direction is corrupt unless both dimensions are unavailable. Independently legitimate partial recipe-identity and batch-metadata subsets remain capability-gated. `CurrentSchemaValidationRejectsExactZeroPairsAndAcceptsArithmeticUnavailablePairs` directly rejects degraded fan-out subsets and inverse arithmetic shapes while retaining reducer-produced states.

`DegradedResourceFanOutSubsetPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` and `DegradedCurrencyFanOutSubsetPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` each create a valid six-unit-resource, 150-currency craft through the production craft/payment/delivery adapter and `NativeProfileCoordinator`, degrade only the relevant capability through the coordinator, stop the runtime, corrupt one primary fan-out level, and leave the backup byte-for-byte intact. A fresh production coordinator rejects the corrupt primary, reports recovery, preserves the original save generation and unaffected sibling evidence, selects the intact backup, and repairs the primary. Focused M16 Debug and Release pass 23/23; complete Debug and Release pass 900/900. This deterministic UDS-owned current-schema persistence correction requires no Duckov gameplay or save manipulation and supersedes the prior package/deployment.

### M16 review correction: directional currency arithmetic recovery

The review froze branch head `61192b6e47483d71d9eebabc48d60578bdd54a93` and demonstrated that either currency arithmetic-unavailable flag disabled all zero/nonzero pair validation. The reducer is directional instead. If action arithmetic overflows, action fan-outs stop before mutation while an exact amount fan-out may continue, so `0 actions / positive amount` can be legitimate but `positive actions / 0 exact amount` cannot. If amount arithmetic overflows, amount fan-outs stop while exact action fan-outs may continue, so `positive actions / 0 amount` can be legitimate but `0 exact actions / positive amount` cannot. When both dimensions are unavailable, either earlier frozen shape can remain.

`HasImpossibleCurrencyPair` now enforces that state machine at lifetime, output, and recipe levels. `CurrentSchemaValidationRejectsExactZeroPairsAndAcceptsArithmeticUnavailablePairs` rejects both inverse shapes and retains direct reducer-generated action-overflow, amount-overflow, and both-unavailable controls. `InverseCurrencyActionArithmeticPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` and `InverseCurrencyAmountArithmeticPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` each persist an exact one-action, 150-currency craft through the production callback, adapter, coordinator, repository, and atomic writer; stop the runtime; corrupt all three primary currency fan-out levels plus the relevant flag; and keep the backup byte-for-byte intact. A fresh coordinator rejects the corrupt primary, reports recovery, preserves the generation and six-unit resource evidence, restores one action and 150 currency from backup, and repairs the primary. Focused M16 Debug and Release pass 25/25; complete Debug and Release pass 902/902. This deterministic UDS-owned current-schema correction requires no Duckov gameplay or save manipulation and supersedes the prior package/deployment.

### M16 review correction: saturated zero lifetime resource

The review froze branch head `5e63287a59ecd7da5b8918a3ac30bacabf671eaa` and showed that `ResourceQuantityArithmeticUnavailable` allowed a persisted lifetime resource row with zero consumed quantity. The reducer cannot emit that state. `ApplyResourceQuantity` is the only lifetime-row constructor and receives canonical positive resource mutations; if its checked preflight saturates, the entire quantity dimension is skipped before the constructor runs. Later saturation preserves an already positive lifetime value. Association action evidence may remain independently meaningful, but a lifetime resource row itself must always be positive.

Current-schema validation now rejects every zero lifetime resource row regardless of quantity arithmetic availability while retaining the existing reducer-produced saturation-direction aggregate. `SaturatedZeroLifetimeResourcePrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` creates a valid six-unit-resource, one-action, 150-currency profile through the production callbacks, `NativeCraftingAdapter`, `NativeProfileCoordinator`, repository, and atomic writer; stops the runtime; zeroes only the primary lifetime and association quantities; sets `ResourceQuantityArithmeticUnavailable`; and leaves the backup byte-for-byte intact. A fresh coordinator rejects the primary, reports recovery, preserves the generation, restores lifetime and association quantity six, action count one, and currency 150, then repairs the primary. Focused M16 Debug and Release pass 26/26; complete Debug and Release pass 903/903. This deterministic UDS-owned current-schema correction requires no Duckov gameplay or save manipulation and supersedes the prior package/deployment.

### M16 review correction: impossible exact resource pairs

The review froze branch head `3c554081089ffb3ca3b999d5540b980fed0aebbe` and showed that current-schema validation accepted a resource association whose exact consumption actions exceeded its exact consumed quantity. Production mutations are canonical and positive: every resource consumption action contributes at least one consumed unit. Quantity saturation is the only legitimate exception because quantity updates stop while independently exact action evidence may continue.

Current-schema validation now requires `ConsumptionActions <= ConsumedQuantity` whenever resource quantity arithmetic remains exact, while retaining reducer-produced action-over-quantity states after `ResourceQuantityArithmeticUnavailable`. `ExactResourceActionsExceedingQuantityPrimaryRecoversProductionCraftFromBackup` performs two successful six-unit, 150-currency crafts through the production callbacks, adapter, coordinator, repository, and atomic writer; stops the runtime with an exact two-action, twelve-unit, 300-currency backup; changes only the primary lifetime and association quantities to one; and leaves the backup byte-for-byte intact. A fresh coordinator rejects the primary, reports recovery, preserves the generation, restores twelve units, two actions, and 300 currency, then repairs the primary. The direct validator control rejects the exact inverse while the existing quantity-saturation control retains one action across zero later quantity. Focused M16 Debug and Release pass 27/27; complete Debug and Release pass 904/904. This deterministic UDS-owned current-schema correction requires no Duckov gameplay or save manipulation and supersedes the prior package/deployment.
